# Evidence

What the port recovers from the source, with the action indices, PCs, opcodes and engine addresses behind it, and which rules are authored for training. [gameplay.md](gameplay.md) describes how the rules play.

## Recovered and authored behavior

- **Recovered behavior:** `src/game/standing.ts` ports the verified standing interpreter, including variable/collision-record assignments, branching, unsigned LCG arithmetic, timing accumulator, explicit unsupported-opcode errors, and odd-width facing placement. It executes instructions at fixed 10 ms ticks, independently of Phaser rendering. Idle action-end restarts action 1 with the continuing random seed as a new prototype choice.
- **Acceptance:** Vitest compares every event, branch, instruction, final PC, timer, and seed against 21 independently generated Python traces. The supplied seed-0 handoff timeline is also compared directly: 18 displays, nine images including 295, 223 ticks. These are comparisons against the supplied research; the original Windows game has not been run here.
- **Authored rules:** every move, object and reaction plays from its own source timeline (see Animations), and motion, body push, the hit test, the camera and round flow follow the engine routines cited below. The port authors its controls (the single keys beside the recovered commands, the Shift dash, the guard key, a ground jump on a fresh press, the touch buttons), the fighter, opponent, stage and mode choices, the practice partners and settings, the normal KO path for every lethal strike, and the music toggle; each bullet below names its own authored details.
- **Animations:** `animations.ts` loops locomotion/crouch, plays finite action and reaction sequences, and holds final airborne/ending poses. Recovered costume changes are preserved. Timing is authored where source commands wait for gameplay. Ryuko's normals (178–194), Hit Up (200–202), dashes (13–17), Jump 2 (10–12), specials and supers (see Supers) play from their own flattened timelines, as Zoma's moves do (see Ryuko's moves): Drill Punch 207 and Air Drill 208–212; the axe throw's “FIRE START” (226), whose object is “Fly Ball” 227 with impact 230; IORI 246–248; DST 214–224 with its missile objects 218; the chuck 196–199; EX Ch 204–205 and CPU IORI 410–411. Aerial drill follows the contact-dependent stages of actions 209–211, each spin's hit and guard handlers entering its next, rather than playing a fixed number of spin cycles; a spin of 211 that passes without contact jumps back to 210 (PC 3060). Jump, dash, and drill movement use recovered opcode-1 velocities at source speed 10, converted to the web art scale. Air-dash entry preserves airborne velocity until the first motion command (PCs 205/223); the original action-change path at `0x410d50` clears motion only on the ground. Opcode `0x1e` controls action cancellation eligibility, not dash acceleration. Per-contact fighter pauses use recovered response timings described below. Neutral, guard, victim and result states keep clips the match drives itself; the normal KO uses the recovered motion described below. Kick 3 now uses the source BEAM hit and no-time-lack guard pose sequences (83–84, 163, 165, 167). Victim reactions follow the recovered responses and motion described below. While its axe (slot 6) lives, flying or breaking, axe throw 226's op `0x04` at PC 3267 jumps to action 225, which shows one display and returns to neutral, so the axe flies on; PC 3279 would likewise jump to punch 178 rather than spawn a second, which can't happen once 226 has started without one. With EX mode's player variable 0 at 10, PC 3266 enters EX Fire (231) instead (see EX mode). Sixteen ticks in, 226 spawns action 227 at (−6, −102) (PC 3279). Action 227 moves at raw 900 (PC 3293) and re-sets box (63, 3, ±39, ±44; kind 6, damage 30) every 3-tick display; handlers 2 and 3 (hit, guard) enter impact 230. Action 227 loops until deleted: `0x4124c9` deletes an object that doesn't follow its parent once it leaves x −50..1330 or y −50..1010 source pixels (the floor is y 920, `0x412247`), the DST missiles too. After each display, 227's op `0x1f` (PCs 3297–3318) checks global 6 (`0x4456bc`, selector `0x86`): while it is above 0, the axe zeroes its motion and box and replays that display. Supers and COUNTER HIT add their freeze to global 6 beside their op `0x1a` stops (Getter Beam 10, then 65 at age 5), and KGT action 58, the stage layout, counts it down every tick except while paused (PCs 366–371), so an axe holds from its next display through the first after the freeze. Each tick the engine lists the live objects by priority (`+4`, 128 buckets, slot order within one; `0x404d0b–0x404d3f`) and runs their scripts in that order (`0x404d51–0x404d89`), before the hit test and motion; the draw walks the same lists, lowest first (`0x404df1`). Op `0x04` sets a child's priority from flag bits 0–1 (`0x412b83`): one below its spawner (10 at least), one above (127 at most), byte 13, or the spawner's own; a contact's effect takes `0x5d` (`0x40f5d3`). KGT objects spawn at `0x65` (`0x406890`'s callers), after the fighters (`0x50`) and the axe and missiles (op `0x04` flag 1, one in front of their spawner; 0 behind, 2 from byte 13), so the countdown follows every read. The DST missile (218, PC 3168) holds one display only when global 6 is exactly 1, and that tick's motion leaves it in place. The port keeps the engine's order (`0x404d4c`, then `0x40ffc0`): every script step, then the hit test, which meets fighters and objects where the last motion pass left them and applies its contacts (`0x40ffdc`, `0x40ffe1`), then the motion pass (`0x40f910`, `0x40ffe6`), which skips a stopped object (`0x40f963`) and moves the rest by the velocity their steps just set. It steps the objects below the fighters' `0x50`, then the fighters, then the rest, and draws the first behind the fighters and the rest over them: a hit effect's streaks (flags 4, `0x5c`) and the DST dash smoke's puffs (`0x50`) draw over the fighters, and Getter Beam's glow (flags `0x21`, `0x51`) spends a stop it shares with Ryuko from the tick it arrives, resuming a tick before her. `0x406570` zeroes a new object and runs no script, and the lists hold only the objects alive at the tick's start, so an object a step or a contact spawns first steps, and first draws, in the next tick's pass. So Getter Beam's hitbox, spawned at age 95, strikes at 96; the axe and EX shots set their motion and boxes the tick after their release; a hit effect plays its sound and spawns its streak a tick after the contact, and the streak rolls its pose set a tick later still. Shortcut: within a priority the port keeps spawn order, where `0x406570` takes the first free slot. Every next action goes through one field (`+0x38`), which a step enters first, stopped or not, running its records instead of the landing check, commands and CPU (`0x412327`). A contact queues the victim's reaction there (`0x40f541`), a box with damage first clearing both stop counters (`0x40f692`, `0x40f754`), so a stopped victim or striker moves in that tick's motion pass; a guard queues the striker's guard handler (`0x40f6f1`), an object's hit its hit handler (`0x40f88c`), and the pass after the motion a player's hit handler while nothing else is queued (`0x40fffd`); op `0x14` queues its reaction (`0x413136`), clearing the victim's stop, and a loss that empties life the lose action (`0x40e84b`). The last write wins. So a contact's reaction, its op `0x1a` stops and the striker's handler all start at the next step, and a striker without a handler that steps before its victim steps once more first. A step counts a stop down and ends (`0x412390`), and the motion pass skips only an object still stopped after the steps (`0x40f963`), so a stop a step adds holds that tick's motion, and the tick it reaches 0 moves by the velocity the object kept. The axe's slot-1 box and slot-0 body (PCs 3290–3291) repeat its box 2000 pixels down, where only objects meet: `0x40f010` tests every object's boxes against the other side's bodies, and an object victim (`+0x15a` 1) only fires the striker's hit handler (`0x40f36a`, taken up at its next script step) and marks the striker as having hit (`+0x15e` bit `0x10`, `0x40f31c`). `0x40f010` checks that bit only before a striker's pass, and every display step clears it for an object holding a box of kind bit 1 (`0x41257d`), players included, and op `0x18` setting such a box clears it for a player (`0x412d49`). In Ryuko's data that layer holds the axe's box and body, the beams' boxes (270, 277: (644, 1995) ±(644, 72)), the Mega Getter Beam's lunge box (292: (59, 1908) ±(40, 89), hit handler 294 from PC 4311) and its beam's bodies (303, 304: (0, 1995) ±(10000, 182)). So two opposing axes break into 230 together, without damage, stop or effect, even while held; that beam's bodies break every opposing axe; its lunge enters 294 on an axe; and a beam, which sets no handler, only stops hitting until its next box. The lunge records the axe as caught (`+0xdef9`, `0x40f2e0`), but 294 has no op `0x07` or `0x14`: only grab-entered actions (197–199, 215–223, 257–259, 320–322) and Air Drill's hit branch (209, PC 2997) hold or toss, so the axe flies on. A script that runs out reaches the next action's op `0x00`, which deletes an object (`0x41273c`, `0x413bc6`); `0x4124c9` also culls one that leaves the world unless it follows a parent. Back in neutral, `0x410ff1` enters the action word of the round's result (`+0xe007`: 1 win 57, 2 lose 59, 3 draw 65), and the result states (`0x409444`, `0x409519`) give the loser 3 once the timer (`0x470050`) has run out, so a time-out loser takes the draw action, WIN STAND and the seated pose. Turns use actions 23/24 (ten ticks, mirrored mid-turn pose). The Vs. CPU intro follows action 30's path for global 3 = 0 (one human player, PC 286): action 32 jumps to action 33 at PC 333, skipping the blank repositioning displays. Raw forward velocity 200 lasts until PC 339 (50 ticks, about 150 arena pixels), then the transformation poses finish at 199 ticks. Its walk-in endpoints, 360 pixels apart around the stage center, and skipping the intro in free practice are authored. Win and draw call WIN STAND (action 58, 115 ticks) through opcode `0x09` at PCs 565/667 before their own poses. Crouching kick response 7 selects action 79: the victim holds three 2-tick impact poses, launches at raw velocity −300/−1020 with gravity 90, and lands into knockdown 146 and get-up.
- **Hit Up:** action 200 supplies the upward kick, with 15 startup ticks, 10 active ticks, and 50 recovery ticks on a miss/block. PCs 2813–2815 set horizontal velocity 600 plus acceleration 350 for six ticks, then stop. A hit enters action 201 through the hit handler (op `0x02` slot 2, PC 2821); a guard has none. After its first five-tick display, its op `0x24` (PC 2837) checks once for any up (`0x200b`) in the last 20 ticks, held or not, and enters action 202; its op `0x1e` (PC 2832) admits only a command for 202, which none is. Action 202 prepares eight ticks, then sets forward velocity 320, vertical velocity −1530, and gravity 42. Ground/crouch response 19 selects victim action 81: self/other pauses 10/33 at PC 932, a 25-tick impact hold, then velocity −160/−1700 and gravity 60 at PC 937. Air response 17 selects action 80: pauses 13/13, six impact ticks, then velocity −600/−1220 and gravity 90. Both wait for actual landing before action 146 knockdown and the existing get-up. Airborne victims remain hittable for follow-up attacks. Motion uses the same recovered fixed-point scales as jumps and dashes. Its op `0x17` responses (19, 19, 17, 25, 26, 17) give a standing or crouching guard its guard reaction (154, 156) and an air guard the air hit (80). Lethal contacts take the normal KO path, a training rule (see Ryuko KO). These are static-source reconstructions, not original Windows playback verification.
- **IORI:** the source command named IORI enters action 246, with follow-ups 247–248. Display durations give startup/active/total ticks of 21/8/59, 23/8/61, and 21/5/71. Raw forward velocities are 800 at ages 13–20, 700 at 8–22, and 800 at 8–20 respectively (about 95.947, 157.413, and 155.914 unobstructed arena pixels). Each stage's op `0x24` checks (PCs 3625/3628/3630, 3661/3663/3665, at ages 29/49/54 and 31/51/56) jump to the next stage: `0x413a50` only sets the PC, with no action change, so the contact counter carries over, and once a stage connects the next accepts a super from its first tick. Ground hit responses select 75, 121, then 80: the first two pause both fighters for 15 ticks, hold nine reaction ticks, then slide away/pull inward with raw velocity −650/+650 and acceleration +10/−10. The finisher pauses 13 ticks and uses the existing outward launch. Ground guards use stop reactions 159/161 for the first two strikes (ten-tick contact pause, nine-tick pose hold, velocity −250/acceleration 8), then the existing strong block. Against an airborne victim, the first two use the existing strong air reaction approximation with 15-tick pauses. The finisher selects response 77 → action 123: 15-tick pause, nine-tick hold, then velocity −500/+2000 and gravity 100. Floor contact enters 124, holds ten ticks, bounces with velocity −400/−700 and gravity 70, then enters the 90-tick floor recovery from 147 and get-up 145. Each stage sets `0x1f` player variable 6 to 100 (PCs 3606/3638/3673), which routes a super cancel to its SC version, and clears it as it ends (PCs 3633/3668/3700); neutral (PC 2), every hit reaction, NO SUPER (PC 3715) and action 254 (PCs 3756–3769) clear it too, so the super entry reads the variable itself. Complete victim scripts remain deferred.
- **Body push:** after each object moves, `0x40fce9` (in the motion loop `0x40f910`) walks its body slots from 19 down and, for every kind-bit-0 box overlapping one of a later object's, pushes both apart by a quarter of the overlap (`<< 14` in 16.16), in whole source pixels (`x >> 16`). Against a grounded fighter only the airborne one moves (`0x40fec3`), a KO'd fighter (life `+0xdf05` is 0) pushes no one, from the tick a contact empties it, and hit stop skips motion but not the push. Held, holding and hidden poses (the held victims, 197, 215, 222, 294 and the shocked victim) set no push box, so those pairs overlap. A walker shoves an idle opponent along at half its speed; the drill's forward box (44, −74) ±(38, 86) holds its victim about 164 arena pixels ahead, and kick 3's leg box (57, −56) ±(57, 65), set before its eight strikes (PC 2617) and cleared after them (PC 2622), shoves a victim on about 21 pixels as the first strike opens, and a guarding one again at the second. The motion loop takes fighter 0, then fighter 1: each moves, adds its transfer and clamps (see Corner push-back), and fighter 0 pushes in between, against fighter 1's boxes from the previous tick. Fighter 0's share stays unclamped until its next motion step, and a box fighter 1 opens meets fighter 0 a tick late. An air dash's fall (action 7) pushes with its own box (−3, −141) ±(39, 52) (PC 96), which meets a cornered body only once the faller comes down to it, 53 ticks into the dash (getting up against the wall, the walker is eased back 13.5 pixels at once).
- **Corner push-back:** a hit sets the victim's reaction bits (`+0x15e`) to 8 and a guard to 12 (`0x40f7a8`, `0x40f6a2`). A strike by a fighter's own box records the striker in the victim's `+0xdefd` (`0x40f8a4`), and so does an object following its parent (op `0x04` flag `0x20`, `+0x28` bit `0x20000000`, `0x40f8ac`); other objects leave it as it was. Action changes (`0x410cf0`) keep both, through a reaction's follow-ups such as knockdown, bounce and get-up. A script that runs out reaches the next action's op `0x00`, which returns the fighter to neutral (`0x4126de` → `0x411a80`) and clears bits 4 and 8 (on the ground 1 and 2 as well) and the striker (`0x411abc–0x411b06`); landing without a handler clears the bits too (`0x4124c3`). A command entered while free (bits 4 and 8 clear) sets 4 and clears 8 (`0x411a41`), as does landing into a handler such as LEND (`0x4118f3`); a cancel keeps them (`0x4119e8`), and so does op `0x0a`, which air attacks, air dashes and airborne supers use to fall into action 7. A player's motion step adds its transfer (`+0xdf49`, `0x40fa09`), then clamps it to the walls (`0x40face`); when the wall stops a victim holding both marks and not KO'd, the stopped distance joins the transfer of the striker's owner (`0x40fb08`), unless the striker is itself reeling from a hit (bits 8 without 4, `0x40faf0`). An object striker's bits stay clear, so a cornered Getter Beam victim pushes Ryuko back, and her beam (objects 269/271/273, 282, 324/327/328, all flag `0x21`) moves with her: after its own motion, a following object takes its parent's position plus the op's offset, mirrored by the parent's facing (`0x40f992–0x40f9d9`). The striker moves by it at its own next motion step, the same tick for fighter 1 and the next for fighter 0. A cornered kick or guard therefore ends at the open-stage gap with the striker giving up the slide, and a victim launched into the wall pushes the striker away through its bounce and floor slide. In the Vs. modes the walls are 50 and 590 source pixels from the camera (see Stage/camera), and the left one measures the stopped distance as (50 − camera) − x (`0x40fbdd`) rather than from the wall, so once the camera has scrolled it hands on nothing. A contact sets the bits only when its box deals damage (byte `+0xc`), so a grab (196, 214) records the thrower as striker without them. Ops `0x07` and `0x14` then mark the held or tossed victim reeling and held (`+0x15e & ~5 | 0xa`, `0x413020`, `0x41314a`) and keep the striker. Op `0x07` places the victim once, before its display, unless its bit 4 (`0x10`, DST's fast spins 217) has the holder's motion step re-place it every tick (`0x40fa29`), after the holder's transfer and before its walls. Neither op `0x07` nor `0x14` clamps the victim; its own motion step does, so a victim moving earlier in the pass than a re-placing holder (fighter 0 held by fighter 1) shows unclamped until the holder takes the overflow. A cornered thrower is therefore pushed out as the spins swing its victim behind it into the wall: by the 15 and 75 pixels the −10 and −60 holds lose, after which the −60 hold meets the wall exactly.
- **Hit effects:** a hit or guard by a box with nonzero damage (r[12]) spawns the effect action in its response entry's second word (player `+0x6dac`, read at `0x40f567`): 339/341 (burst 345 with small stars), 340/342–344 (burst 346 with a big star), 370 (guard flash), 377 (the DST missile's explosions) or 406 (beam hit 407). `0x40eab0` places it at the centre of the attack box's overlap with the struck body box, and `0x406570` creates it as an object (type 4, z `0x5d`) facing the striker. Both bursts spawn streak 348, flash 347 and stars 351/360 as ordinary objects, drawn additively and tinted by op `0x23` (mode 2, offsets −14, −5, +7). Op `0x14` responses (throw and super holds) run no hit test and spawn nothing. Effects render above the fighters and expire after their last frame, including during KO; simultaneous hits keep separate effects. Objects roll op `0x20` (jump to r[6] at offset r[8] when `rand() % (r[1] + 1) > r[3]`) from the match's shared rand: streak 348 plays pose set 348, 349 or 350, each mirrored at offset 17 (PCs 5147, 5163, 5179, 5195, 5211), and stars 351/360 fly one of four ways (352–358, 361–367). Fighters roll it from the same rand as their next step enters the queued reaction, before the hit effect's objects roll (the fighters' bucket `0x50` runs before the effects' `0x5d`): in 75, 76 and 80 the branch only skips a sound (PCs 794, 819, 909), and in 83, 84 and 122 it swaps the first three poses (PCs 957, 982, 1619, offset 18). CPU IORI rolls its recovery as 410 ends (PC 5887). Each display a fighter enters also runs the op `0x04` spawns (and op `0x03` sounds, see Sound) its action makes just before it, whether the port draws it from a clip or a flattened timeline. The script steps into a display before the hit test, so a fighter enters it as it moves, a queued reaction or handler included; an action the tick's end starts (a released hold) enters its first display then. Spawns include: landing smoke (9, 85: 400), hit and guard dust (394, 395), knockdown dust (396), slam object 402, the air dash's 390/391, kick 3's 380, the drill's, IORI's and the throw's smoke, and the KO's flash. A reaction that rolled its op `0x20` first runs the records before the roll, those of its own first display. An op `0x04` without flag bit 2 (`0x412a88`) first deletes the owner's object in slot r[12] (with action 0, that is all it does), then registers the new one there (`0x412caf`); an object's deletion clears its slot (`0x40e4a0`). So punch 2's and kick 2's dust (flags 0, PCs 2394, 2586) replaces whatever holds slot 0, and the first display of every hit reaction (73–135, including the held 128 and the tossed 129) deletes its fighter's registered objects 7–9, the beams (spawned with flags `0x21`). Shock (88) deletes only 8 and 9; the KO (60) and the guards delete none. Hit effects come from `0x406570` directly and take no slot. Flag bits 0–1 put an object just behind its spawner, just in front, at z r[13] or with it (`0x412b83`), where the fighters are `0x50`, and r[4] is the record the object starts at (`0x412c86`): the KO (60) spawns 59 at record 9 (PC 600), panel 294 as a viewport object at z 15, behind the fighters, added at +25 for ten ticks (PCs 592–593), so the court flashes. The DST missiles (218) and the axe (227) stay projectiles with their own rules. A nonzero r[5:7] instead jumps the spawner to that action while the slot's object lives (`0x412ac0`); the export doesn't decode it, since only the axe throw uses it and the match checks its axe at the command.
- **Attack boxes:** every normal strikes with its op `0x18` boxes (centre ± half size in source pixels, x toward its facing), active from the display that sets each box until the one that clears it. Punch and kick: ages 7–11 (PCs 2367, 2559). Crouching punch 14–23 (PC 2541), crouching kick 18–27 (PC 2732), air punch 18–32 (PC 2477). Air kick: three 4-tick boxes from age 15, 8 apart (PCs 2649–2657). Punch 2: three 3-tick boxes from 18, 6 apart (PCs 2401–2414). Punch 3: 181 + 18's ages 14–18 (PC 2454), after its two hops land, 34 ticks after it starts. Kick 2 16–25 (PC 2595), Hit Up 15–24 (PC 2824), IORI 21–28, 23–30 and 21–25 (PCs 3620/3656/3690). Kick 3: PC 2619 calls action 190 eight times, a 3-tick beam box (169, −115, ±163, ±25; PC 2632) every 6 ticks from 15. The drill: six 3-tick boxes from 23 (PCs 2921–2942). Air Drill's spins keep one box (29, −54, ±60, ±39; PCs 2985/3015/3036/3057), re-armed by their contact stages. A box connects when it overlaps the victim's body box on both axes, the test the super boxes use. Against an idle victim's body box (−1, −92, ±37, ±102), the punch reaches about 260 arena pixels centre to centre, the kick 282, the air kick 149 and the beam 552. Each box's own damage (r[12]) and kind (r[10]) apply, as below. Authored: the impact effect positions.
- **Body boxes:** op `0x19` (`0x412d63`) stores slot r[9] of the object's body table (`+0xd9`); a zero size clears it. An action change through `0x410cf0` clears the body and attack tables (`0x40e550`); op `0x09` calls and op `0x0a` jumps keep them. The hit test (`0x40f010`) checks each attack slot (`+0x89`) against every body slot whose kind has bit 1 or 2 set (`0x40f1b8`); kind 1 alone only pushes. Both axes must overlap, each box mirrored by its owner's facing. The exporter attaches the boxes set at each display, and a pose that sets none keeps the previous pose's. Idle keeps action 1's box (PCs 10, 24). So crouching (action 19, PC 237: −2, −71, ±32, ±83) and walking (±28) narrow the body; a backdash (action 14) keeps only a low box during its wind-up and none once airborne; a grab (196) keeps only a push box on its grab tick (PC 2748); knockdown (146) keeps one below the floor; and get-up (145) and FLY NO HIT (97) have none. The DST missiles meet the tossed victim through the same test. Grabs meet body boxes through the same test (see Throws). Nothing else protects a fighter: a held victim's script stops at action 128's first display, which clears its body (PC 1732), and a slam bounce (124) can be struck once its second display sets a body box at (9, −33) ±(67, 32) (PC 1668).
- **Damage:** Ryuko's parameter block (player offset `0x7ca5` onward) holds 20% chip, the 30% desperation threshold, 60% damage taken there, 0% combo decay and 510 life. On contact, `0x40f010` reads the attack box's damage D (r[12]; none if 0). A guard takes max(1, D × 20 / 100) only from a box of kind bit 2 (4). A hit takes D less D × decay × the victim's hit count / 100 (nothing for Ryuko), then max(1, that × the struck body box's r[11] / 100). The first overlapping body slot wins. r[11] is 100 on most poses, 120 on Ryuko's attack poses, and 110 in one of punch 3's (action 181). `0x40e7c0` applies every loss, op `0x15`'s included: at life ≤ 153, a loss becomes max(1, 60% of it), and life reaching 0 KOs, through a guard too. Normals: punch 10, kick 8, crouching punch and kick 30, air punch 15, air kick 4 × 3, punch 2 5 × 3, punch 3 35, kick 2 20, kick 3 4 × 8, Hit Up 15, IORI 15/18/20, drill 5, 5, 5, 5, 7, 8, Air Drill 20/5/3/1 by stage. Chip-capable kinds: IORI and the drill 7; Air Drill, the axe and most super boxes 6 (the hidden super's finish 4, the Mega Getter Beam's opening 5). Throw 30 (PC 2770), DST toss 10 (PC 3112), each DST missile 22 (PC 3164), the axe 30 (PC 3292). The per-hit power words (`0x7cbe`/`0x7cc0`) are 0. Authored: the DAMAGE counter includes chip.
- **Victim responses:** every attack's timeline carries its own op `0x17` response ids, selected by stance and guard as the super boxes are. A victim crouches for them in a crouching action or with down in its input word (`0x40f4be`): this tick's, or, while a stop's flag holds, the word that op `0x1a` stop saved (`0x413676` self, `0x413715` other; read at `0x40f2ff`), which the victim's next step neither stopped nor held clears (`0x4123c5`). Each id maps through the response table to a victim action and that action's op `0x1a` stops for victim and attacker; punch 2 and the drill switch sets before their last strike (PCs 2413, 2941). Air punch and kick use responses 5/6 → action 77 against standing and crouch-guarding victims, and 28 → 159 against a standing guard. Punch 3 uses 77 → 123 for every stance except a standing or air guard. Ground normals and Hit Up answer an air guard with hit responses (2, 4, 7, 9, 11, 17). The drill's last strike uses 18 → 80, and Air Drill uses 56 → 135 (PC 1841: velocity −800/+2000, gravity 100, landing handler → 124) and guards 34/35 → 163/165, or 80 → 172 in the air. Its first hit branch (209 + 12) then re-applies 56 at (100, −20) through op `0x14` (PC 2997), so a grounded victim rises in front of the drill into its later stages. PC 2996 would skip that while global 10 is 10, but only KGT PCs 542 and 590 write global 10, to 0. A victim takes one reaction stop per tick however many boxes enter its reaction: the last box's reaction is queued, and its op `0x1a` runs once. Each reaction runs its op `0x01` records at their ages from its entry; the grounded ones move after their impact poses. 73 moves at −280 for ages 5–24 (PCs 758–760) and 75 at −650 with +10 from age 9 (PC 800). 77 moves at −250 with +10 from 9 (PC 845), and 149/151 at −320 from 8. 154/156/163/165 move at −650 with +8 from 9, and 159/161 at −250 with +8 from 9. The engine integrates `vx += ax; x += vx` without clamping (`0x40f96a`). 83 moves at −150 with +10 from its entry (PC 954); it stops at its 25th tick of motion and drifts back in until the 34-tick reaction ends. The air reactions play the op `0x01` commands of their flattened scripts; those at age 0 run as the reaction enters, and the motion pass moves by them from the tick its stop runs out. 74 and 76 rise at −100 (PCs 774/816), then launch at −300/−650 and −300/−850 with ax 1 and gravity 66 at ages 5 and 6 (PCs 778/823). 84 drifts at −100 for nine ticks, then launches at −400/−830 with gravity 80 (PC 990). Guards 153, 158 and 167 move at −420 or −450 with ax −15 and gravity 70; at ages 3/6 a flag-`0x1a` command zeroes vy and keeps the rest (PCs 2063/2136/2266). Op `0x20` in 83 and 84 only picks one of two pose orders (PCs 957/982), so both last 34 ticks. Each response entry's second word names its hit effect (see Hit effects). None of these reactions has a landing handler (op `0x02` slot 1, player `+0x64`). On landing, `0x411810` zeroes the motion and sets the action to −1; without a handler it turns the fighter and clears the state flags, so the free state reads commands on the landing tick. That check opens the step (`0x41187f`: y at or past the floor while vy is positive), and the motion pass has no floor, so a fall reaches the floor in one tick's motion and lands at the next step, drawn up to a tick of motion past the floor between. A reaction that runs out in the air reaches `0x411a80`, which enters the character's fall action (word 6 of the block after the CPU table, player `+0x7586`: action 7). Its op `0x01` (PC 99, flags `0xe`) keeps vx, vy and ax and sets gravity 80, and its landing handler enters LEND (9).
- **Commands:** `src/game/commands.ts` ports the recognizer. Every tick, frozen or not, `0x4146d0` records each player's input as a word (`0x414340`): bits 1 back, 2 forward, 4 up and 8 down, then K `0x10`, Hit Up `0x20`, P `0x80` and Chuck `0x100`. Left and right count from the opponent's side; `0x40e6c2` flips the current word when that changes, and Ryuko's flag byte (`+0x7cb6`, 2) lacks bit 8, so the checks never mirror. The loader reads 23 command entries of 82 bytes into player `+0x4da2` (`0x403b64`): a name, a window of history ticks (`+0x20`), the actions entered airborne, near, far and crouching (`+0x22`–`+0x28`) and ten u16 steps (`+0x2a`). A step's low nibble is its direction (0 any, 1 none, 2–9 exactly F, DF, D, DB, B, UB, U or UF, and 10–13 any back, up, forward or down, `0x410c74`/`0x410cac`); bits 4–9 are buttons that must all be held, none of them on the tick before; `0x2000` marks it, and bits 14–15 select a type: only ZIRO's TWIN SHOT uses one, type 3, a turn of the stick (below; `ponytail:` the held types 1 and 2, `0x4102ed` and `0x410473`, aren't ported). The free state (`0x411a01`) and each op `0x1e` window (`0x41195a`) run the scan at `0x410060`. In table order, an entry matches when its last marked step falls on a tick within the window and each earlier step on an earlier one. Steps marked `0x3000` with direction 0 thus take a tick each between directions, so a direction must be held a tick before its button. Matching an entry whose window exceeds 29 clears all but the last 20 ticks (`0x41073e`). Airborne the entry needs an air action, and none is read within 50 source pixels of the floor on the way down (`0x410096`). On the ground and still, it takes the crouching action while down is held now, else the near action within 110 source pixels of the opponent (`+0x7ca3`, `0x41080e`) or the far one. An entry without that action lets the scan go on. A cancel window then refuses a command outside its levels. Ryuko's motions: ↓→ P throws the axe (`fire`, 40 ticks), ↓← K drills (40), ↓← P starts IORI (40), ←↓→ K grabs (`DST`, 45; far, DST FAR 220), ↓ ↓ Hit Up switches EX mode (`----TYPE`, 20), ↓→↓→ P and K fire Getter Beam and the Mega Getter Beam (`G BEAM LV1`, `B G`, 50), ↓←↓→ P enters the hidden super (50), and →↓←→↓← P and K enter Musashi and the Change Attack (90). The Hit Up and Chuck buttons, P and K are one-step entries (10). Jump 2 (entries 10–12: neutral, then exactly U, UF or UB, 10 ticks) has only air actions, the double jumps 10–12. DASH F and DASH B (13, 14: F, neutral, F or B, neutral, B, 13 ticks) enter dashes 13/14 on the ground and air dashes 16/17. A Jump 2 or DASH still in its window matches first, so a refused one holds back later entries: Ryuko attacks out of an air dash only once its first tap is 13 ticks old. The CPU clears its history every step it runs (`0x411307`) and writes the steps of the command it enters, the last at the current tick (`0x4115e3`). Its cleared ticks read as neutral, so holding an up direction in the air is Jump 2. Authored: the port's command keys (U, I, G, F, E, 1–5, and Shift for DASH F, or DASH B with back held) clear the player's history and write their command the same way, a turn as F, D, B and U. The guard key and a fresh up press on the ground come before the scan, where the source's `0x410dc0` jumps while up is held. Two flags stand in for the windows that refuse movement until landing: double jump 10's (PC 130, levels 1–254) refuses Jump 2, and action 7 entered from an air attack, an air dash or a script (PC 94, 0–250) refuses both; an air reaction's end frees the fighter (`0x411a80`). Hit Up's chase jump (202) lifts off at PC 2848, after that tick's scan.
- **Chains:** op `0x24` (`0x413a50`) runs the command check at `0x410a50` and, on a match, enters the named action at the given offset. Byte 4 is a window of input-history ticks, followed by up to five u16 steps. Each step has a direction nibble (0 any, 1 neutral), buttons in bits 4–9 that must be freshly pressed (P `0x80`, K `0x10`, Hit Up `0x20`), and `0x2000` on the last step. The check finds the newest last step, then earlier steps in earlier ticks, all inside the window. Punch 1 and kick 1 enter 179/187 through handler slots 2 and 3 (hit and guard). Those check at age 8 (windows 20/25) for neutral then P → 180 or K → 188. Punch 2 checks at ages 21, 27 and 33 (windows 10, 10, 30) and kick 2 at 26 (30), for P → 181 or K → 189. These checks read the command history below; each runs in its action's timeline and jumps like op `0x0a`. Punch 2's lunge (PC 2389, raw 600 until PC 2393), kick 2's (PCs 2584–2592) and punch 3's hops come from their op `0x01` records. Punch 3 hops at raw 300/−400 with gravity 110 (PC 2434), and its landing handler (slot 1, PC 2433) jumps to 181 + 11 after 12 ticks: the motion pass (`0x40f910`) runs after every script, so a fighter moves on the tick its op `0x01` runs, and lands at the step after the motion that reaches the floor. Two ticks later it hops at 300/−300 with gravity 120 (PC 2440), landing after 8 into 181 + 18 (PC 2439), then slides at raw 500 from PC 2447 until PC 2457, about 187 arena pixels in all. Its strike opens 36 ticks after it starts, and its 30-tick display at PC 2460 brings it to 79 ticks. IORI's stages check at ages 29/49/54 and 31/51/56 (PCs 3625/3628/3630, 3661/3663/3665; windows 30, 30 and 20) for any down, any tick, any back, then P, entering 247 or 248.
- **Cancels:** op `0x1e` copies its bytes into the player at `+0xdf91`. In a non-free action, `0x41193a` reads its mode. Low bits 1 accept a command only once the cancel counter at `+0xdf85` exceeds 1, and 2 always. The command's action must have a level (byte 2 of its first record) within the op's bytes 2 and 5, or, when bits 3–5 are 1, be the action in bytes 3–4. Op `0x18` arms the counter for a box with kind bit 0, the contact handler at `0x40eec4` raises it to 2, and an action change clears it. Levels are 21–22 for normals, 31 for specials, 41 for supers, 42 for `B G`, 30 for the forward dash, 0 for the backdash, 254 for air dashes and 255 for the double jump, so a connected normal cancels into a forward dash but not a backdash. Ground normals, punch 2 and kick 2 accept 30–50 after contact, and 179/187 accept it always. Air punch accepts 22–255 and air kick 30–255 after contact; 183/184/192/193 cap both at 50. IORI's stages accept 32–41 after contact, which a jump to the next stage keeps (see IORI). Crouching down and standing up (18, 20; PCs 233, 246) accept 0–50 always. Air dashes accept 20–50 from age 4: the record at age 3 (PCs 207/225) runs in that tick's script step, after the controller (`0x411810`, called at `0x41251d`) has read the old window, so commands meet it from the next tick (`0x412564`). COUNTER HIT accepts 30–50 after contact until age 50. Variable 1 (`0x1f` selector `0x41`) is 10 after actions 10–12 and 20 during 16–17; a dash's end jumps into action 7, which clears it. Air punch and air kick (PCs 2464–2465, 2637–2638) then enter 184/193 or 183/192, whose falls keep it (op `0x0a` to 7 + 2); 183 and 192 set acceleration 45; every air attack's jump into 7 then restores gravity 80 there (PC 99). The controller (`0x411810`) runs on every step a stop doesn't skip (`0x412390` returns first), so a window reads commands on each unstopped tick, and a press made during a contact stop enters on the first tick after it if it is still within its command's window.
- **Guard counter:** guard reactions 149–165 run op `0x10` (no stock enters twins 150–166 without the checks), then op `0x24` for command `0x2020`, the Hit Up button. Light guards check at ages 0/8/18 (windows 8/10/10), the others at 0/3/6/9/19 (10, 10, 10, 10, 13), entering COUNTER HIT (174); the match reads each fighter's checks from its own guard reactions' timelines. It runs as a flattened script. It takes 100 power and stops the opponent 10 ticks, then 20 more at age 5, and spawns 347. At ages 15–24 its box (0, −100, ±100, ±100) deals 10 source damage through responses 17 → 80. Its kind `0x47` includes `0x40`, which zeroes the guard decision (`0x40f469`), as does Musashi's 30-damage finish (257, `0x46`), so neither can be guarded. Air guards (153, 158, 167) have no checks.
- **Daisetsuzan Oroshi:** command entry 6 (`DST`, ←↙↓↘→K) selects action 214. Its one-tick grab box (67, 6) ±(70, 1), kind 8, at action age 4 (PC 3091) meets an idle body box up to 173 source pixels centre to centre (about 259 arena pixels), through the normal grab's test (see Throws). A miss plays the 58-tick recovery. A grab branches to action 215, which calls throw spin 199 three times and fast spin 217 ten times through opcode `0x09` (PCs 3107–3108): 185 ticks in all. Response 30 first places the victim 60 source pixels ahead. Before each spin display, opcode `0x07` repositions the victim at 60, −10, −60 or 0 source pixels. Flag bit 2 turns the victim to face the holder's way and mirrors the image; bit 0 draws it behind the holder. Handler `0x412e93` replaces the victim's display with held pose 1 (image 47, offset −84/−159) from the character's 15-entry held-pose table. That table is read into player offset `0x70ca` and exported as `held`. The handler also sets the victim's `+0x40` to −1 (`0x41303a`), replacing any stop count; until op `0x14` clears it (`0x413147`), the victim's steps return at once (`0x412397`), so its action age holds. A stop added meanwhile would free it (op `0x1a` adds to `+0x40`), but the only stops a holder gives its held victim, the Change Attack's (320, ages 275–387), each follow an op `0x14`. The −1 also sends the renderer to its held path (`0x40d6a0`–`0x40d76d`) instead of the normal character formula. There the offsets start from the image's top-left corner: left = x + dx, top = y + dy. When the holder's facing XOR flag bit 2 mirrors the image, left = x − dx − width; flag bit 3 flips it vertically with top = y − dy − height. Pose 1 therefore stands on the court at the holder's side, and Musashi's pose 2 hangs from Ryuko's raised fist. PC 3111 then applies response 31: the victim is placed at (14, −96) and enters action 129 at raw velocity −200/−3000 with gravity 90. Op `0x14` queues the victim's reaction (`0x413136`), so when Ryuko's script runs first (fighter 0), the victim's step enters it later the same tick and launches at once. Action 130's spin loops until landing, then PC 1756 bounces it at −100/−1000 with gravity 80 through loop 131 into knockdown. From PC 3120, Ryuko slides at −400 for 25 ticks. PCs 3125–3130 spawn two exhaust flames (385), dust (396, 399), and two missiles (218) at (30, −80) and (71, −78). Each missile moves at raw 1000/−1000 from its first step, the tick after. The first one's box (−6, −21, ±47, ±29) meets the falling victim's (−1, −92, ±37, ±102) 18 ticks after that, the hit test taking it where the last motion pass left it, for 22; the second, 61 arena pixels further out, misses, so the throw deals 10 + 22. Every response is 32 → action 85, and the missile's hit handler enters 384, which deletes it. The missile hit stops the victim for 13 ticks and launches it at −500/−1700 with gravity 90. Spin loop 86 continues until landing, then PC 1011 bounces it at −200/−1200 into loop 87 and knockdown. The spins' displays also spawn the recovered smoke puffs (394/395, 398/399), and each missile its smoke ring (219, PC 3160). The G binding and meter-free access are training rules. EVIL DST: opcode `0x11` at PC 3082 selects action 221 at life ≤ 153 of 510. It repeats 214's box and 58-tick miss with images 321/325/31 and credits the same 8 power. Its hit handler enters 222: response 30 at 60, then `0x07` holds poses 1, 8, 12 and 6, and op `0x09` calls 223 five times; each call spawns HP hit 340 (playing 346) and landing smoke 400 and credits 5 power, with no damage. At age 165, PC 3233 jumps to DST Hit 215 at offset 4, re-applying response 30 before the normal spin; the flattened timeline follows the jump. DST FAR (220) sets its shorter box at age 4 (PC 3189) and, unlike 214, 221 and 224, never clears it, so it grabs through its whole 58 ticks. The dash smoke (396) puffs smoke 394 behind itself at ages 10, 15 and 20, 20, 30 and 40 source pixels on (PCs 5705–5710), from its own timeline.
- **Power gauge:** Ryuko's parameter block (file offset `0x157a1d9`, read into player offset `0x7ca6`) gives 510 life, 100 power per stock, three stocks, none at the start, and the 30% desperation threshold (153 life). Opcode `0x15` credits power in each attack's own script: the normals and Hit Up at their records' ticks (punch 3 +3, then +10 twice after its hops; kick 3 +2 per strike), the drill, IORI and DST when they start (+8, +8/+3/+3, +8) and in each reaction script to both fighters: weak hit 3/2, strong hit or launch 5/3, rapid hit 1/0, and blocks 1–5 to the defender only. A throw adds 5 to both fighters. A grounded axe or EX shot hit answers response 50 with FB (110), which adds 10 to the thrower (PC 1442) before 75's 5/3. Routine `0x40e6f0` carries full gauges into stock and borrows stock back when power falls below zero. HUD object types 20/21 (`0x40de1b`/`0x40de44`) draw power × 126 / 100 pixels of the gauge, keeping the texture's right-hand columns (mirrored for 2P). KGT actions 69/70 shimmer the gauge (images 31–38), 45–48 and 74–75 show the EMPTY/100/200/300 stock digits, and 104/105 blink UNDER PW (135) at 30% life or less. The blink rate, its placement at the outer ends of the port's life bars, and additive drawing are authored.
- **Supers:** command entries `G BEAM LV1` (air 263, ground 264), `MUSASHI` (256), `CGA` (319), `hidden sup` (286) and `B G` (292) select the five supers. `scripts/export_assets.py` flattens each action and every object it spawns. It inlines opcode `0x09` calls with their repeat counts and follows `0x0a` jumps. A jump back to an executed PC is a loop; a jump into a basic action ends the timeline. It exports displays and gameplay opcodes at their action ages: `0x01` motion, `0x02` handlers, `0x04` spawns, `0x07` holds, `0x0b` calls, `0x10`/`0x11` checks, `0x14`/`0x17` responses, `0x15` life/power, `0x16` input checks, `0x18` boxes, `0x1a` stops, `0x1f` checks of player variables 0, 1, 6 and 12 and global 6, and `0x23` colour. Each super is the action of its command entry and runs as the generic `'script'` action, so its entry checks (`0x1f`, `0x11`, `0x10`) jump in script order as any move's do. A stock at or below `r[6]` enters NO SUPER (251, or 250 in the air: 40 ticks, the lettering object 392, 10 power at age 30). Otherwise stock changes by the signed `r[7:9]`: −1, or −3 for `B G`, which needs three. Life at or below 153 selects G BEAM EVIL (278/280). There, object 282's final box does 25 instead of 5. The hidden super enters the axe throw (226) at life 153 or more. Ops `0x1a` stop the opponent at entry, for example 10 + 65 ticks for Getter Beam, and each super spawns SUPER STAR 371 (374 for LV3). At age 95, Getter Beam spawns muzzle 271, beam 273 and hitbox 269, which first steps and strikes at 96. Object 269 calls 270 nineteen times: a fresh box (644 ± 644, −5 ± 72 from its origin) every 10 ticks, damage 5, with responses 51/51/76 → 111 → 83 and 122, guards 34–36 → 163/165/167. Its final box selects 58 → FBH 112, which adds 10 power to Ryuko, → 80. The air beam hovers until its gravity-60 fall at age 350, and its landing handler (PC 3964) enters Dash F lend (15). Musashi moves at raw 200 for ten ticks; its grab box (44, 10, ±63, ±10), kind `0x2a`, opens at ages 52–61 with response 30 everywhere, passing over an airborne opponent or one in a guard reaction, and handler slot 2 enters 257 (578 ticks). The IORI cancel version (260) adds a slot-1 box (60, −130, ±60, ±55) of kind `0x18`, which takes only an airborne opponent; `0x40f010` tries each slot in turn. There, `0x07` lifts held poses 1 then 2 overhead at (105, −210); power explosion 386 spawns 387/388 bursts at ages 543 and 573. At age 574, response 43 places the victim at (105, −50) in FLY NO HIT (97) for 120 life, and a 30-damage box follows, which finds no body box: 97 sets only a kind-0 one (PC 1181). Ryuko then jumps into 97 herself: both fly at −100/−2400 with gravity 90 and spin in 98 until landing. The hidden super shows pose 448 for 59 ticks, then calls strikes 287, 289, 288, 289, 288, 289 and ends in 290 (270 ticks). They advance at raw 800/700/500 with damage 30, 13, …, 30, responses 20 → 83, 76 → 122, guards 78–80 → 168–172, and a response 18 finish → 80 (guards 25–27, so 154 standing). Action 122 drifts at raw −100 without falling, launches at PC 1628 (−400/−830, gravity 80), and its landing handler (slot 1, PC 1627) enters knockdown 146; its op `0x20` only reorders poses. The 'time stop' guards have no op `0x1a` and no slide: 168/170 hold in place with COUNTER HIT checks at ages 0/3/6/9/19 (PCs 2275–2308), and 172 falls from rest at gravity 70 (PC 2323). `B G` widens its box at ages 90, 100 and 110; response 33 holds the victim in HIT SHOCK INFIN (88, looping 90) and its hit handler enters 294 (823 ticks). There, Ryuko turns to blank display 8191 while copy 295 flies away, anime frames 329–335 fade in through `0x23` mode 4, and rings 315, anime beam 296 and stone groups 307 spawn. Object 301 spawns viewport-space objects 302/305 (`0x04` flag `0x40`) and hit controller 303: 39 five-tick boxes 10000 pixels wide, then a response 43 finish for 40 life. `CGA` grabs with a box (20, 4, ±10, ±1) of kind `0xa8` (skipping an airborne, guarding or reeling opponent) at ages 45–89 and enters 320: the DST spins (199 × 3, 217 × 10), response 31's toss at age 185, the transformation, and a jump at raw 2500/−3500. Action 321 follows: a second freeze, held poses 8/9 in the air, response 17 (→ 80) for 40 life, a dash back at −2000/−800, a third freeze, and beam objects 327/328/324 (nine 6-damage boxes with response 76, then 58). It falls at gravity 60 until landing. Each hit reaction deletes its fighter's registered objects 7–9 (op `0x04`, action 0; see Hit effects), so a hit cancels a beam. Authored: keys 1–5, releasing a shocked or held victim once its holder leaves its moves' timelines or is KO'd, image 0 as a blank marker, and children of viewport objects starting at the matching stage point. Super cancels from IORI follow the `0x1f` checks of variable 6: Getter Beam enters 265 (EVIL 279) with its own two-stock check; Musashi, the Change Attack and the hidden super enter action 254 at offsets 0, 14 and 9. That action enters NO SUPER below two stocks (and, for the hidden super, at 150 life or more), spends two, spawns GENGAI! (253) and jumps to 260, 318 or 286 + 5. Op `0x23` (`0x413a07`) sets a blit mode and signed offsets that `0x40d070` adds to each 5-bit palette channel, clamped: mode 1 averages with the screen, 2 adds and 4 fades by `r[5]`/32 (blitters at `0x40c840`; mode 3 subtracts but no Ryuko record uses it). Action changes keep the setting (`0x410cf0`), and Ryuko's neutral, knockdown and win actions reset it at their start (action 1 at PC 11), so a fighter keeps a super's tint through a hit until then. Objects set it from their timelines: DST smoke turns half transparent (219, 394–399) and the DST fire adds with offsets 0, −6, −7 (385); the axe impact's clip halves its second display (PC 3337). Op `0x25` (`0x413a9a`) gives an object an afterimage slot (`+0x151`, 100 samples at `0x447f80`), or frees it when `r[3]` or `r[4]` is 0. Before its script each tick, frozen or not, the object counts down and, past zero, restarts the count at `r[4]` and samples its position, facing and display (`0x412271`), so a sample lands every `r[4]` + 1 ticks. The renderer then draws the last `r[3]` samples, oldest first, just before the object, in blit mode `r[5]`; `r[6]` keeps the object's colour (0), takes `r[7:11]` (1), or blends from `r[7:11]` toward the object's as the image is newer and the count higher (2; `0x40cd15–0x40d4cf`). Action changes keep the slot. Backdash 14 draws three half-blended images every six ticks, air dashes 16/17 two fading magenta ones until a landing (9) frees them, the drills and IORI three, and the hidden super, Change Attack, hit stars, rings and the B G objects theirs. Op `0x0e` (`0x4132fa`) shakes the screen: `r[9:12]` and `r[12:15]` give the x and y shake's type, amplitude in source pixels and length in drawn frames, and each record restarts the axes it names. The draw routine (`0x404dd0`), which the main loop runs once per drawn frame after that frame's ticks (`0x405c0a`), first steps each axis through `0x40c9e0`: the offset decays (type 1), grows (2), holds (3) or is `rand()` % amplitude (4), flips sign on odd drawn frames (`0x4456fc`), and the last frame zeroes it. World objects then draw at their position less the scroll and the shake (`0x40d82f`); viewport objects and the HUD stay put. The port steps it once per browser frame. Hits and launches (79–81, 85, 123, 124, 129, 146, 147), punch 2 and 3, kick 3 (a 1-pixel, 80-frame rumble), Hit Up, every Air Drill contact, DST, the KO, COUNTER HIT and the supers shake. A clip display carries the op `0x0e` and `0x25` records run just before it, and the match runs them as a fighter enters the display. Authored: type 4 rolls its own generator, because the source's draws from the shared rand once per drawn frame, which would tie the simulation's rolls to the display rate. Op `0x0e`'s `r[1:9]` also starts colour faders. Flag 1 starts the owner's (`0x4dfd93` + player × `0xe03f`) from the running object's colour toward `r[2:6]` over `r[6:8]` ticks. Each tick ends counting the faders down (`0x404d97`), and while one runs the renderer (`0x40ca90`) writes its blend over r, g, b and fade of every object of that player (types 0, 1 and 5 at `0x40e42c`; hit effects are type 1 objects of the striker, `0x40f5fc`), so its last write stays. Type 1 blends; type 2 also shows the start colour on odd drawn frames. Musashi Hit flashes Ryuko toward green +30 over its additive draw for 30 ticks (PC 3849), then fades her back over 10 (PC 3857), leaving g +2 until a later op `0x23` resets it; LV 3 Hit fades her objects to 0 (PC 4394). Not implemented, since none can show with Ryuko's data: flags 2, 4 and 8 start their faders from zero, not from the actor's colour (`0x41336c–0x413481`). No Ryuko record uses flag 2 (the caught opponent's fader), flag 8 (the global fader, for type 2 objects) or type 3's random blend, and flag 4 (the screen fader, for type 4 objects) appears only in the round intro (PCs 303, 327), fading from 0 to 0. Every super sets player variable 14 to 100 (op `0x1f` selector `0x4e`; Getter Beam at PC 3975, clearing it at age 80, PC 3990), and most other actions clear it before their first display. The round intro (actions 30/32, PCs 300/325) spawns action 43 'Black' for each player as a viewport object at (327, 498) in bucket 15, under every other object. Each tick, before the fighters' scripts, it checks the variable (PCs 428, 433): at 100 it shows image 294, a 673 × 507 panel of palette entry 0 ((0, 0, 8), which is not the colour key), opaque for a tick (PCs 431–432) and checks again (PC 433), staying opaque while the variable holds; once it doesn't, PC 434 sets it to 0 and the panel shows five ticks at 50% (PCs 435–436), so the court darkens behind the fighters and fades back. The port gives each player that object from the start, with or without the intro. Op `0x1f` checks of globals 3 and 10 keep their fall-through path (see Throws and Victim responses). The CPU enters supers through the same commands.
- **EX mode:** command entry 15 (`----TYPE`: ↓, neutral, ↓, then the Hit Up button 0x20) enters EX Ch (204, level 31) on the ground. Its ops `0x10` and `0x15` (PCs 2858–2865) add 50 power when no stock is left, then take 50 twice once that makes a stock, or once and enter NO SUPER (251) when it doesn't; with a stock they take 50. The second op `0x10` lacks `r[5]` bit 0, which makes it jump on enough stock instead and pay nothing (`0x4135eb`). So it spends 50 of the gauge's total, or enters NO SUPER below 50. PC 2866 then enters NOR Ch (205) while op `0x1f` player variable 0 is 10; otherwise PC 2867 sets it. NOR Ch clears it (PC 2880). Both show image 366 four times for five ticks, offset −30, −20, −10 and 0 (op `0x23`), and spawn EX Obj 375 at (12, −159). The round intro's action 33 checks down (op `0x16` condition 7) at PC 348, once the display at PC 347 ends (intro age 162), and jumps to 'Chage Character' (37), which sets variable 0 and jumps back (PCs 400–401). Action 30's check at PC 307 lies on the two-player path. The HUD face, action 70, runs before the fighters (bucket 20). In EX mode it shows image 2002, 2001, 2000 and 2001 for 40 ticks each, counting from the tick after the variable is set (PCs 712–730). Otherwise it shows 2001. Op `0x16` (`0x412da7`) jumps to `r[2:4]` + `r[4]` when condition `r[7]` holds, or when it fails with `r[1]` bit 0. The conditions (table `0x413ce8`) read the input word: 1 on the ground, 2 and 3 there without or with down, 4 forward and 5 back (by the facing at `+0x5c`), 6 up, 7 down and 8 no direction. Op `0x0b` (`0x412a2f`) calls an action, and running out returns after the call (`+0x85`, `0x412625`). The step loop skips op `0x00`, so a jump to an action's offset 0 just runs on. EX Fire (231) first jumps to 225 while slot 6 holds an object (PC 3340), and clears variable 12. After 13 ticks of windup it enters EX Fire Cyuu (237). Each of its displays ends in an op `0x16` condition 4: holding forward takes the jump to the next, and releasing falls through. Releases in 237 (ages 18–51) jump to 231 + 13. That recoils at raw −100 and spawns 233 at (−6, −102) into slot 6 (PC 3355), and recovers for 30 ticks. 237's last two checks (ages 55 and 58, PCs 3475 and 3478) and 238's (ages 61–77) enter EX Fire 2 (232), which sets variable 12 to 10 (PC 3364) and spawns the same object (35 ticks). 238's end jumps to EX Fire Genkai (239) at age 78. It shakes x (type 4, 8 pixels, 10 frames), recoils at raw −1000 and spawns EFG Ball 240 at (−6, −122) (PC 3519). Object 233 jumps to EX Ball 234. Before each three-tick display it calls 228 through op `0x0b`, and 228 calls 229 instead while variable 12 is 10 (PC 3321). 228 moves at raw 800 with box (53, 3) ±(49, 44), kind 6, damage 30, plus its copy 2000 pixels down; 229 moves at raw 1000 for 40. After each display the shot clears its motion and box. While global 6 is above 0 it then replays the display (234 + 8, …), as the axe does. Its bodies sit at y + 2003 and y + 5003. Its responses are 50, 50, 4, 25, 26 and 27, and its hit and guard handlers (op `0x02` slots 2 and 3) enter 235. That clears variable 12 (PC 3438), spawns 244 100 pixels ahead and shows a blank display for ten ticks. Response 50 enters FB (110), which credits the striker 10 power (op `0x15`, PC 1442) and enters 75 on the ground (PC 1444) or 76 in the air (PC 1443). EFG Ball 240 flies at raw 1500 through 241, spawning trail 242 every nine ticks. Its box (100, 0) ±(79, 75), damage 50, has copies at y 3000 and 4000, its bodies sit at y 2000, 3000 and 4000, and its responses are 58 (FBH, 112 → 80) and 25–27. Its handlers enter Ball Hit (243), which drifts at raw 1000, then 100 and 50, for 30 ticks. So a full-charge shot breaks small shots and axes and passes through them, and only another full charge breaks it. The match keeps one object box per object, the slot-1 copy at y 3000; the slot-2 one at y 4000 meets nothing else. Objects take their op `0x1f` checks of global 6 and variable 12, and their handlers, at once. A looping script keeps its object until a handler, the world bounds or a slot replaces it, and each pass runs its records again from the loop's target. In EX mode Getter Beam's first check (PC 3970) enters EFS (331), ahead of its variable 6 (PC 3972) and life (PC 3974) checks, so it replaces EVIL too; the air beam (263) has none. EFS checks variable 6 (PC 4918) for EFS SC (332), then spends a stock, or two for EFS SC, which spawns GENGAI! (253) itself; with fewer it enters NO SUPER. Like Getter Beam it darkens and freezes for 10 + 65 ticks. It charges through EFS SF (333) eight times, calls EFS Att 1 (334) four times and jumps to EFS Att Final (335). Each recoils at raw −1000 and spawns an EFS Att Ball: 336 flies at raw 1500 for 20 with responses 51 (FBB, 111 → 83), 76 and guards 34–36. The final 337 answers 58 (FBH, 112 → 80) and guards 25–27. Both enter Ball Hit (243) on contact and replay through the freeze like EX Fire's shots. Getter Beam's and EFS's op `0x1f` checks of variables 0 and 6 (an IORI stage) jump in script order with their op `0x10`/`0x11` checks. Authored: the E key beside the motion command.
- **Sound:** op `0x03` (`0x4132c8`) plays sound `r[2:4]` from the running script's character table (player `+0x221c`, 42-byte records: name, payload size at +36, type at +40). Objects use their owner's. `0x403430` plays type 1 as a DirectSound WAV, looping with bit `0x10`. It first stops and rewinds every buffer of that sound (`0x415f00`), so a sound restarts rather than overlapping itself; different sounds mix. The export writes the 45 WAVs Ryuko's exported records cue, as stored (8-bit mono, 22 050 Hz), and the match lists each tick's cues with their player. Clips and flattened timelines carry them on the display after the record, like spawns: punch 1's swing (39) plays as its striking display starts (PC 2364), its hit effect 339 plays LP HIT (31), and kick 3's beam (190) plays 26 every repeat. A heavy hit's op `0x20` (75, 76 and 80, PCs 794, 819 and 909) jumps past its voice (14), so the port plays the records before the roll and then the branch. The axe impact (230) plays 34 (PC 3334). KGT action 26, the KO lettering, plays KGT sound 1 ('KO', PC 180) as its seventh display starts (tick 31), and the round calls play theirs (see GUI/HUD). Music: after loading a stage, `0x40677c` plays the first word of the stage's 0x409-byte block (`0x4042d2`): Sport Court's 1, '235no wav', and Train Morning's 2, 'no sound.wav', both silent, as are the other sixteen stages' (the same two files, or Street Night's 13-millisecond 'No.wav', which sits at the midpoint). Stage actions 2 and 3 play Sport Court's 75-second track (sound 2) only once global 14 leaves 200, and only SIN's, KAY's and DIDO's supers set 200; Train Morning's are the same records with its 139-second track as sound 1, and each other stage guards its track with the same check (Greek's actions 3–6 play sounds 1–4). KGT 57's 162-second track (11) needs global 0 above 99. So the original plays no music in a Ryuko or Zoma match on these stages, and by default neither does the port. Authored: the **Music** checkbox above the arena loads the stage's type `0x11` track (`sounds/<stage>-<sound>.wav`, such as `sounds/court-2.wav`, 'Sprotmorning.WAV'; Greek has two tracks, and the port plays sound 3, which its 'off' action (3) plays, over the 71-second sound 2) on first use and loops it, restarting with each match as a stage load would. Also authored: Phaser's Web Audio plays the cues (the browser unlocks it at the first input), and pausing pauses them.
- **Throws and recovery:** action 196 (CHUCK) sets a one-tick grab box (35, 6) ±(41, 3), kind `0xa8`, at age 7 (PC 2750), with responses 30 and hit handler 197 (slot 2). `0x40f010` tests a grab box against body boxes as it does a strike, and for every box first skips a player victim by its kind (`0x40f12d–0x40f18e`): `0x10` one on the ground, `0x20` an airborne one, 8 one in a guard reaction (`+0x15e & 0xc` is `0xc`, set at `0x40f6a2`) and `0x80` one in a hit reaction or hold (8, set at `0x40f7a8` and by op `0x07` at `0x413020`). Free (0) and attacking (4, `0x411a41`) fighters stay grabbable, and DST's kind-8 box also takes a hit reaction. Against the idle body box (−1, −92) ±(37, 102), the grab reaches 112 source pixels centre to centre (about 167 arena pixels). A turn (23/24, PCs 252/255) ends its body box at y −1, above both grab boxes, so a turning fighter cannot be grabbed. The grab tick keeps only a push box (PC 2748), so it cannot be struck and simultaneous grabs miss. Action 197 applies response 30 at (60, 0) (action 128), calls throw spin 199 three times with its op `0x07` holds and smoke (PC 2767), then applies response 31 at (14, −96) and op `0x15`: 30 life and 5 power to each fighter (PCs 2769–2770). Those 65 ticks match DST Hit's first spins, and both play their own timelines: op `0x14`'s responses 30 and 31, op `0x07`'s holds and op `0x15`'s damage, then the toss (129–131) and knockdown (146, get-up 145). The holder's motion step re-places the victim every tick only while the last op `0x07` has flag bit 4 (`0x40fa29`: 217's fast spins, Musashi's lift), and op `0x14` clears those flags (`0x41314c`). Op `0x07` zeroes the victim's motion (`0x412f66`) and op `0x14` keeps it, and neither holds the victim above the floor: Zoma's 253 holds Ryuko as far as 52 source pixels below his feet (age 215). Its recovery displays last 140 ticks, 205 in all. PC 2761's op `0x1f` (`0x4137a4`: selector `0x83`, mode 8) jumps to Chuck Hit ZA (198) while global 3 (`0x4456b6`) is above 0. Only the 2P cursor of character select (KGT 85, 87: image 171, the red 2P mark) writes global 3, setting it to 50 (PCs 541, 548, 585), and the engine spawns that cursor only when side 1's controller presses a button (`0x407814`). So 198, and the matching branches of DST (216, PC 3109), Musashi (258, PC 3875) and the Change Attack (322, PC 4774), are the versions for two human players; the port has no second human, so its throw correctly stays on 197. The throw's player variable 3 (30, PCs 2762/2771) is read only by object 38, which nothing in Ryuko's data spawns. Neither fighter's Chuck command entry has an air action (ground 196 for Ryuko, 200 for Zoma), so neither throws in the air. Authored: releasing the held victim when the thrower is hit or time runs out, set on the floor if held below it.
- **Stage/camera:** the engine's world is 1280 source pixels wide, viewed 640 at a time; the port scales both by 1.5. The mode global `0x470058`, set from the title menu (entries enabled by `0x4438a4` bits 4, 8 and `0x10` give modes 0, 1 and 2), picks the camera and walls; the port plays as the Vs. modes 1 and 2. Every tick the scripts run before the hit test and motion (`0x404d6f`, then `0x40ffc0`), and among them the camera object (`0x40af30`, state 3) aims 320 source pixels left of and above the fighters' midpoint (truncated, the floor at y 920) and moves halfway there, truncating (`0x40b022–0x40b080`). The scroll is then clamped to 0–640 and 0–480 (`0x40b2cc–0x40b314`). It therefore rises once the pair's midpoint is 120 source pixels off the floor, and keeps moving through KO and round endings. A player's motion step then clamps x to 50 and 590 source pixels from that scroll (`0x40fba2–0x40fca2`): the fighters stay within 540 source pixels (810 arena) of each other, a stage end's wall is 50 pixels in, and sprites may cross the screen edge. Mode 0 instead clamps to 50–1230 and scrolls by stage flags (`0x4d9a55` bit 4) and objects of type 4, unless the object has a wall handler (`+0x70`, which a wall touch moves into `+0x38`). Authored: the camera starts on its aim rather than round start's 320, 480 (`0x407094`), which is the same for fighters centred on the stage. HUD, KO lettering, and timer stay fixed to the viewport.
- **Stages:** `export_stage` reads all 18 stage files (hash-checked). Each round's setup (`0x408c3f`) calls `0x406610`, which makes an object (type 4, at 0, 0) of every named action between the first and the last that holds a display: z `0xc`, behind the fighters, or `0x64` from the first action of kind 3 (`+0x23`) on: Sakura Night's spawners 28–31 and Sakura Street's 32–35. Its first step keeps its start record (`0x41221d`), whose r[1] bits 3 and 4 scroll it by the camera times r[2:4] and r[4:6] percent (`0x40da94–0x40db80`): 100 moves with the stage, 0 stays on the screen, Space's and Space Night's actions 6–9 scroll at 85 across, Japan Street's 4 at 70, Park Morning's 3 at 50 across and 130 down, Sakura Street's 9 at 70 both ways, and Street Night's 7 at 80 and 9–11 at 95 across. The draw puts the display's top left at the object plus its offset (`0x40da41`), so Sport Court's halves are images 0 and 212 side by side (actions 5 and 6). Objects step with the others by priority, rolling op `0x20` from the shared rand: Sport Court's seven dancers (8–14) each pick one of two dances at their first step (PC 65 and alike, `rand() % 3 > 1` enters offset 26), before the fighters step. An object that runs out after a display restarts its first action (`+0x34`, `0x4127b7`): Train Morning's TV (6) every 640 ticks, two of its passengers (8, 12) when their rolls fall through, and its train (7) after 1730 ticks: 1008 on the empty track, 222 passing through and 500 empty again, unless the roll that ends them (PC 88, `rand() % 6 > 1`) holds the empty track another 500. The motion pass moves a stage object like any other (`0x40f910`), but its step skips the landing check and the world bounds (type 4, `0x4123cb`), so Train Night's and Japan Street's scenery slides and the parks' and Street Night's bobs. What a stage object spawns is a stage object too (type table `0x413cd0`, `0x412c40`): on the stage whatever flag `0x40` says (`0x412b38`), in no slot (`0x412a7b`, `0x412c99`), placed and layered like any child, and scrolled by its own start record. Sakura Night's spawners (21–24, 28–31) and Sakura Street's (25–28, 32–35) drop petals (2–5) as their rolls pick, and a petal drifts until its op `0x05` deletes it (action 2 after 365 ticks): op `0x05` ends a player's action but deletes any other object (`0x412617`), where running out restarts a stage object (`0x413ca0`). Sakura Street's action 17 can roll a jump 255 records on (PC 159, `rand() % 6 > 4`), past its end, so the step runs out at once (`0x4125ea`) and the object restarts that tick. The match runs their colour (op `0x23`), motion (op `0x01`), spawns (op `0x04`) and rolls; the export refuses any other record, and their op `0x1f` checks (of globals 10, 11 and 14 among them) keep their fall-through path. A stage's image 0 is its background, not a blank marker. The draw adds the shake (`0x447dad`, `0x447dc1`) whole to every stage object, whatever its percentages (`0x40da94`), where world objects subtract it (`0x40d82f`), so the scenery shakes opposite the fighters.
- **GUI/HUD:** the arena uses the source 640×480 aspect ratio at 1.5× scale. `hud.ts` renders the silver frame, life gradients, timer digits, portrait strips, win marks, and energy frame from `SCWU.KGT` and each fighter's container. Screen anchors come from KGT actions 60, 71–77, 103, and 106–112. Life bars crop the original gradients; the power gauge and stock digits are described above. The round manager (`0x408c24`–`0x408e41`) spawns its letterings through `0x4068e0`, which returns the wait in each one's header word (r[1:3]), from the KGT's system table (file tail, loaded at `0x435474`: round letterings at `0x4451c6`, fight at `0x4451da`, KO at `0x4451dc`). Round setup spawns the round-start animation (KGT 13) and counts its 350 ticks. On Vs. CPU's path (global 3 is 0, PC 72) it spawns 115 (PC 94), which shows the game's logo (image 182) for 30 ticks and wipes it away over 39. Then the round lettering (15, 'DRESS 1') plays KGT sounds 7 and 3 ('ROUND1') and jumps to R1 (90), image 111 added to the screen for 190 ticks, and the manager counts its 280. Then the fight lettering (25, 'SHOW TIME', images 121–136, 60 ticks) plays sounds 2 ('FIGHT') and 8, and the manager sets round phase 1 (`0x408e41`), so commands (`0x410069`), the CPU (`0x41130c`) and the timer start 631 ticks after setup. At round end the Vs. modes' result states (300, then 510–540: 'draw', '1p win', '2p win', 'w ko') all show the KO lettering: KO (26) after a win by KO or time, DRAW (32) and DOUBLE KO (33) jump to it, and 1P/2P WIN (30, 31) and PERFECT (27) show nothing. Each state credits the round to the winner, or to both for a draw or double KO (`0x40948a`, `0x40955f`, `0x409394`, `0x40966b`), and counts its letterings' header waits from the KO lettering's spawn: KO's 240 and WIN's 410, plus PERFECT's 0 and one tick when the winner's life is full (`0x4094f4`), or DRAW's or DOUBLE KO's 300. The tick after, state 900 (`0x4096c0`) spawns KGT 14, whose 116 (PC 103) wipes the logo back in (images 187–183 and 174–181, three ticks each) and holds it (PC 821, image 182). When its 50 ticks run out, state 902 (`0x40970a`) ends the match once either side has won the rounds to win (`0x470048`), which Vs. single play reads from GAME.INI's `Editor.TestPlay.VSSinglePlay`, 3 (`0x414afc`, `0x4087da`); otherwise it returns to state 100 (`0x408970`), which counts the round (`0x470044`) and sets it up again the next tick. Round setup spawns the round's lettering from the word at `0x4451c4` + round × 2 (`0x408d70`): KGT 15–19, DRESS 1–5 (images 111–115), voicing 'ROUND1'–'ROUND3' (KGT sounds 3–5) after sound 7. The fighters re-enter at full life (`0x411ca5`) with their variables cleared (`0x4121cc`), so EX mode ends, but only round 1 resets stock and power (`0x411f2d`). The HUD (type 13, `0x409d82`) lines up a mark per round to win from the KGT 76/77 headers, 1P from (240, 40) stepping −35 and 2P from (370, 40) stepping 35: KGT 56 (112, image 30), switched to 55 (112 + 4, image 29 at +1, +1) as each win is credited (`0x40a584`). Authored: the match ends where the source wipes to its post-match screen, the final round skipping that wipe so the ending poses stay visible, and free practice stays a single round. Each lettering draws its displays at their offsets on the 640 × 480 screen in its op `0x23` blit mode. The browser toolbar, name placement, infinity label, post-KO hold, and the result's wait for each fighter to take its result or lie KO'd, are authored. There is no blurred result modal. Original-game recording parity and screen effects remain unverified.
- **Ryuko KO:** a loss that empties life (`0x40e7c0`) sets the KO action from Ryuko's `+0x75a0`, the lose action 59 ('負け'), in place of the response's reaction (`0x40e83e`), and drops the fighter to z `0x3c`, behind the other fighter (`0x40e8cd`). Action 59 deletes the fighter's objects 7–9 and clears player variables 6, 8 and 14 (PCs 584–589), then jumps to action 60, KO Normal (PC 591). The lethal strike's reaction never runs: the loss writes 59 (`0x40e84b`) over the pending action (`+0x38`) where the hit test had put the reaction (`0x40f541`), so it adds no contact pause and no op `0x15` power, FB's credit to the striker included. KO Normal's opening poses hold both fighters 40 ticks (400 ms) at PC 611, after five action ticks; PC 607 also adds ten pause ticks to the other fighter. At action age 14, PC 617 launches backward with raw velocity −500/−1520 and gravity 90. The 25-tick launch pose then holds the falling pose until actual floor contact. Action 62 supplies the ten-tick backward slide (raw speed −200), followed by the lying pose, with no get-up while the round is over (PC 645). An unobstructed grounded KO travels about 420 arena pixels in flight, rises 309 pixels, and slides another 30. Both facings, airborne KOs, simultaneous KOs and projectile endings use this sequence. KO Normal also spawns the court flash (59 from record 9, PC 600) and shakes the screen (PC 602). KO Normal's body box from age 10 (PC 613) can still be struck, since combat runs on after a KO: a fighter struck again enters 59 and then KO NORMAL 2nd (61), because player variable 3 is above 40 (PC 595) and only a KO sets it (50, PC 596). It has no flash or holds and a smaller shake (PC 624), and relaunches at age 6 with the same velocities (PC 631). So a move's later strikes juggle the KO'd fighter (punch 2's last two, kick 3's beams, the drill). A lethal throw toss KOs the victim where it was tossed, since its op `0x15` (PCs 2770, 3112) empties life after op `0x14` has set the toss. Not played: action 59 enters 'make hikari' (63, the fighter vanishes and falls) when global 0 is 10 (PC 590). The title menu writes global 0 only to 1 (KGT 78, PC 497), and LIZARD's chuck hit lend to 10 for its strike's tick (see LIZARD). Selecting the normal KO path for every lethal strike is a training rule. Original Windows playback parity remains unverified.
- **Zoma:** `export_fighter` reads `02 ZOMA.PLAYER` (hash-checked) through the same loader layout as Ryuko's: 25 command entries, the 83-entry response table, the 15 held poses, the CPU table and the parameter block. His block differs in life (490 at `+0x7caa`, so 147 is his 30%), near distance (0, `+0x7ca3`) and its default actions (`+0x757a`: a HIGH JUMP at 6 moves his back jump to 7 and fall to 8; start 28, win 56, lose 57, draw 63). A response id selects the victim action from the victim's own table (`0x40f539`, `[ebx + 0x6daa]` for the target's player) and the hit effect from the striker's (`0x40f567`, `[esp + 0x18]` set from the striker's `+0x156`), spawned as the striker's object. Pairing the two tables by id gives each of Ryuko's victim actions a twin, and the exporter checks each pair's gameplay records (op `0x01` motion, stops, power, handlers, rolls, display lengths) match once their action numbers, record offsets, headers, mode-0 op `0x1e` and unused set values are set aside; it refuses to export if any comparison changes. The twins that differ are recorded: his guard reactions' counter checks P+K (`0x2090`) at age 18 where Ryuko's check Hit Up, with 10-tick windows where Ryuko's weak guards use 8 (PCs 1908–1913); his crouching guard (151) adds 2 more power (PC 1929); his jump hit (78) rolls op `0x20` at PC 784 (`rand % 6 > 2` skips to 78 + 17); his KOs test and set player variable 3 at 30, KO down global 15 at 100; the launch-down stop moves next to its power, and four reactions differ in display lengths after their motion. So the port plays a Zoma victim through its Ryuko twin's rules (stops, slides, launches, landings, power, KO flow) with his own clips, timelines, rolls and held poses. His idle runs his action 1 in the standing interpreter (four displays, 55 ticks), and his HUD shows face 2001 (action 71, PC 677) and name 340 (action 64, PC 637). Authored: the fighter and opponent choices, the partners he offers (attacking by Ryuko's reach), and Ryuko's walking speed and fresh-press ground jump for him too.
- **Ryuko's moves:** her command table enters P, K (standing, crouching and air), Hit Up, DASH F/B and Jump 2 as their source actions through the generic `'script'` action, as Zoma's do (below): `export_assets.py` flattens every command entry's actions with their levels (header `r[2]`) and her default-action table's neutral states, and each move's own records give its displays, motion, boxes, responses, windows, handlers, power, sounds and objects. Her specials do too: Drill Punch and Air Drill, IORI, the axe throw (whose op `0x1f` enters EX Fire) and its axe, DST with its variants and missiles, the chuck, EX Ch and CPU IORI. So do her supers and COUNTER HIT: each super enters from its command entry and takes its own entry checks in script order, and a guard reaction's op `0x24` enters COUNTER HIT. Playing the data changed some timings from the old hand-built clips. Punch 3's second-to-last display is 30 ticks (PC 2460), not 15, so it lasts 79 ticks with its hops. Ground dashes land through Dash F lend (15), which frees their afterimage (PC 191). An air dash's fall is the dash's own (op `0x0a` to 7), so it keeps facing and pushes with action 7's boxes and the guard key waits for its landing, and one that lands before its fall has no landing handler and ends free. Kick 3's leg push box stays through all eight strikes. A crouching attack that runs out returns to neutral, which clears the stance bits on the ground (`0x411b01`), so holding down crouches through 18 again. Three rules of the generic path came with it: a handler keeps its target's record offset (punch 3's landings enter 181 + 11 and + 18), a timeline's last op `0x0a` enters its target (the air NO SUPER's fall 7 + 3, Zoma's CPU dash jump 343 into HIGH JUMP 6, which it previously dropped), and a command enters a script that moves that tick, from a window or the free state, since the motion pass (`0x40f910`) follows every script step. The specials brought more of the same. The axe (227) and DST's missiles (218) are objects with their own scripts, slots, handlers and bounds. Scripted fighters roll op `0x20` at its record (CPU IORI at age 66, PC 5887). Op `0x10` decodes its mode bit, and IORI's variable 6 decides super cancels. Their changes from the old hand-built rules: Air Drill carries a grounded victim (see Victim responses); IORI's later stages accept a super from their start once one connects; DST FAR grabs through its recovery; and a toss launches the tick it is applied. The grab contact counts toward HITS, as Zoma's grabs did. With the supers, op `0x1f`'s player variables (EX mode 0, the jump variable 1, IORI's 6, EX Fire 2's 12 and the darkening 14) share one table. The air beams now land through their handler into Dash F lend (15), where the hand-built rule forced LEND (9). A super cancel by Musashi, the Change Attack or the hidden super plays action 254's own timeline into its cancel version. A super's hit handler follows a lethal hit as every move's does.
- **Zoma's moves:** each of his moves runs its own flattened timeline as the match's generic `'script'` action (the source action in the fighter's `branch`), so his own records give its displays, motion, boxes, responses, power, sounds and objects. His command table enters an entry's action for the stance (entries 1–20: DST, Shin Spark, SPAKE, EN300, g beam, DRAGON, dragon 2 P+K/K/P, FIRE, BOMB, JUMP 2, DASH F/B, Hit Up, chuck, P and K); a cancel window passes it by that action's level, header `r[2]` (P, K, crouch and air P, Hit Up, chuck 21; air K 22; specials 31; SPAKE and DST 41; EN300 and Shin Spark 42; JUMP 2 255; DASH 0; air dash 254). Authored: his specials and supers take Ryuko's keys, U DRAGON, I FIRE, F dragon 2 P, G BOMB, E g beam, 1 SPAKE, 2 DST, 3 EN300 and 4 Shin Spark, each writing its entry's steps.
