Op `0x1e` (`0x413782`) stores the window at `+0xdf91`, which action changes keep and the return to neutral clears (`0x411b0c`); `0x41193a` reads its `r[1]` bits 0–2 as the mode (1 once the box has connected, `+0xdf85` above 1; 2 always) and bits 3–5 as the type (0 levels `r[2]`–`r[5]`, 1 only the action `r[3:5]`). Op `0x24` (`0x413a50`) runs `0x410a50`'s check (window `r[4]`, up to five steps from `r[5]`) and on a match jumps like op `0x0a` (`0x412968`): the P 1 → P1 HIT → P 2 → P 3 chain, the run's high jump, Hit Up's chase. Op `0x18` with a size restarts the contact counter (`0x412d2b`; `ponytail:` a box without kind bit 0 leaves it at 0, which none of his windows meets). Op `0x02` slots 2 and 3 enter the hit or guard handler at contact, slot 1 on landing (`0x411810`), and an action change clears them (`0x40e510`). His op `0x1f` checks of player variable 1 (10 after JUMP 2, 20 in an air dash) pick the air attacks' forms, and his op `0x16` checks loop the run while forward is held. Entering an action (`0x410d50`) stops a grounded player and keeps an airborne one's motion. A jump keeps the body boxes, so a branch into its own action starts with those its earlier records set. A command holds back later entries while it stays in its window even when refused, so P pressed just after JUMP 2 comes out once JUMP 2's neutral tick leaves its 10. His guard reactions run their own op `0x24` checks (Hit Up at ages 0 and 8, P+K at 18, 10 ticks each) after their op `0x10` finds a stock, into COUNTER HIT (174). The crouching responses follow the victim's input word (down, `0x40f4be`) as well as the port's crouching actions. The chuck catches with response 30 (Ryuko's 128, held), holds through op `0x07`, then tosses with response 41: Ryuko's CHUCK THROW victim action 132 (Zoma's 133, a twin but for Ryuko's op `0x20` sound roll at PC 1787 and object deletes), a launch at raw −600/−1920, gravity 90, from age 6 (PC 1793) into the knockdown (PC 1792), adding 8 power to the victim (PC 1783). Its TOMAHU hatchet (202) flies at raw 1700/−1200 and catches the toss with response 32 (Ryuko's 85, the twist) for 20.
- **Zoma's specials:** FIRE (204) first spawns nothing into slot 1 (PC 2846): a player's spawn into a slot whose object still lives (`0x412ac0`) jumps to `r[5:7]` + `r[7]` instead, here P 1 (178), or else deletes that object; its fireball (205, PC 2858) flies at raw 800 with response 50 (Ryuko's FB, 110) and a kind-4 box of 25, so a guard takes 20% of it. DRAGON (208, air 209) sets player variable 6 to 100 (for super cancels, with his supers), rises at raw −1700, gravity 80 (PC 2915), striking with response 42 and lastly 18, and lands through slot 1 into DRAGON LEND (210). Response 42 is Ryuko's 96 (Zoma's 97): stops of 13, 5 and 3 power, an op `0x20` that only skips its sound (`rand % 6 > 2` for Ryuko, PC 1159; `rand % 3 > 1` for Zoma), and a launch at raw −200/−1620, gravity 70, from age 5 (PC 1163) into the knockdown. dragon 2 (213–215) clears his body box (PC 3017), shows his empty image 2000, moves raw 15000 (P), 30000 (K) or nothing (P+K) for one tick, then jumps into its rising strike (216: 35 damage, response 18, landing into 210). g beam (219) spawns a beam (220) whose kind-7 box of 20 re-arms each display: three strikes of response 51 (FBB, 111). BOMB (222) jumps to BOMB RELORD (229, which clears it) while player variable 0 is 50, or adds 10 to it (PC 3154, op `0x1f` mode 2) and lays three marks (223) at 100, 220 and 340 source pixels; each drops a bomb (227) from 962 pixels up after 260 ticks, falling at raw 1200 with response 50 and a box of 20, which explodes (230) on a hit, a guard or the floor. An object falling to the floor (`0x41241c`, y 920) stops there and enters its slot-1 handler. Spawn flag bit 3 only marks the object for the blitter (`0x412cbb`, `0x40b698`).
- **Zoma's supers:** each runs from its timeline like his other moves. Ops `0x10` and `0x11` (`0x413500`, `0x413164`) are jumps (to target + `r[4]`): too little stock enters NO SUPER (233, or 232 in the air through its op `0x16`), otherwise the stock pays (`r[7]` added, clamped to 0–3); Shin Spark's op `0x11` turns it into BOMB at 147 life or more. Each super sets player variable 14 to 100, which shows his own panel (action 40, image 1009) as Ryuko's 43 does, adds its freeze to global 6 and stops the opponent with op `0x1a`. SPAKE (239, air 238) and DST (251) test variable 6, which DRAGON sets to 100: then they jump into 236 (+5 for SPAKE, +9 for DST), the super-cancel versions, which need two stocks and spawn GENGAI (235); DRAGON's window passes levels 40–41 once it has connected. SPAKE's dash carries its hit box on an object following him (242, 84 damage, response 43, Ryuko's 97). DST catches with a kind-8 box (response 30), spins its victim with op `0x07` holds, tosses it (response 31), juggles it with op `0x14` (responses 21 and 60, Ryuko's 84 and 109) and blasts it with a 120 box (response 43). Op `0x14` reaches whoever the player's box last struck (`+0xdef9`, set at `0x40f2e0`), held or not. EN300 (265) rises, calls down five sun balls (270), freezes again for its cut-in (269), then drops a ball (272) whose landing bursts (273) into 6-damage strikes alternating responses 51 and 59 (Ryuko's 108, a stun), then 43 for 30. Shin Spark (279) dashes at raw 4000 with response 32, then crashes down with a 200 box. Responses 59 and 60 select a stun standing (Ryuko's 108, Zoma's 109) and in the air (109, 110; his last display lasts 20 ticks to Ryuko's 10 + 10), each adding 1 power to the victim and rolling which shaking order plays. A clip display now also carries its op `0x1e`, so each stand (20) opens its 0–50 window from its clip. SC DST (252) would jump into DST past its stock payment (251 + 4) at PC 3509 while global 10 is 10, but only KGT PCs 542 and 590 write global 10, to 0.

- **Practice partner:** `dummy.ts` turns each behavior into input for the partner, so it plays through the same rules as the player. Its behaviors, the one-second attack interval, the 250-pixel jump-in distance, and both helpers are authored for training (Low health holds the player one point under the 30% threshold, since Ryuko's hidden super, Zoma's Shin Spark and G Suneo's hh take their desperation forms only below it, op `0x11` at 153, 147 and 150 or more); its punch and air kick wait until the opponent is within the move's recovered reach; they are not the original CPU. The Original CPU choice runs the recovered CPU instead (see CPU below), guard roll included.
- **Giant:** `export_fighter` reads `07 GIANT.PLAYER` (hash-checked): 18 command entries, 43 CPU entries, 550 life (165 at 30%), near distance 110, and default actions (`+0x757a`) with Ryuko's jumps and crouch, then start 30, win 60, lose 61, draw 67 and HUD face 74; his jumps land in lend (9, PC 41). His victim actions sit two after Ryuko's KOs and lose action, four after her other reactions, five for the twists and the shock (his ★変なＴＥＣＨ, 89, comes before them, hers after) and from Clothes off 500 (his clothes obj 2, 107, comes before it), seven from Pull Back (his UNCOUNTER reactions, 126 and 127, come before it) and six past her Chuck Hiso (139), which he lacks; `GIANT_TWINS` derives them. The response table pairs every twin but responses 69 and 70 (Kiki's En300, ANMC P's jump fire ball), which name UNCOUNTER DOWN and Air Fire (126, 127) where Ryuko's name DOWN and J atttack: copies of his DOWN and J Att that clear variable 4 where those test it. The export records them (`aliases`), checks them against the table, and the match runs them by DOWN's and J Att's rules (`ponytail:` Air Fire's op `0x01` sets vx −250 where J Att's adds it, PCs 1720 and 828, and it counts no variable 5). **Counter:** each reaction the table names opens with an op `0x1f` on variable 4 before its first display: 18 jump to counter hit (279) while it is 100 (PCs 723, 763, ...) and 34 set it to 0 (PCs 744, 788, ...). Counter start (278) holds it at 100 for 72 ticks (PCs 4271–4282), while its main body takes 200% (PC 4268). `export_fighter` maps each reaction to its test's target or 0 (`counters`) and compares the rest with Ryuko's; contacts and op `0x14` now queue the victim's own action from the response, so entering it can take the jump before the reaction's stops and power, or clear the variable. Counter hit adds 10 and then 70 to the freeze (PCs 4294, 4304) and calls Counter run (281) twice. Its hit handler enters Counter hit (282), which grabs with response 30, then hides the victim with response 81 while Counter hit rp (284, called four times, PC 4333) reapplies it among the smoke. Op `0x07` shows them again (PC 4334), and response 43 tosses them for 60 (PCs 4342–4344). In EX mode the counter command enters New movieng chuck (290, PC 4261). Against Ryuko's records, beside that op `0x1f`, his twins differ in: the guards' Hit Up counter within 10 ticks, as Kiki's, with no op `0x1e` window (PC 2160) where Ryuko's opens one for COUNTER HIT (PC 2012); Chung's rand % 3 > 1, or no op `0x20`, in the heavy hits, HISA, the Tatumaki hops and the chuck toss; Kiki's HISA Down at raw −800 (PC 1799); the knockdowns clearing variable 14, not 1 (PCs 2126, 2142); the get-up's 15 and 5 ticks (PCs 2121–2122) to Ryuko's three tens; Zoma's KO tests (PC 577), landing wait and KO DOWN; the shock clearing variable 1 where Ryuko's sets 6 (PC 1042); ANMC P's rand % 4 > 2 in the beam hit and stun; Zoma's rush air hit, air stun, uppercut family and wall flights; FB, FBB and FBH as his own copies of hit heavy, BEAM Stand and HISA (79, 87, 84) where Ryuko's jump to hers, FB and FBH giving the striker 10 power as hers do (PCs 1464, 1528); and his own MUSIC ATTACK (below). **New reactions:** his moves use three responses no earlier move used, each with a twin in every player (`ZOMA_TWINS`; Amiro's also clear variable 7). Response 38, from BASE BALL MISS ATT and ANMC P's EX CHUCK HIT (PC 3265), names 'ヒット Fly twis' (92), which flies as Chuck Twis side (136) with 91's 13-tick stops (PC 1096). ANMC P's toss used to find no reaction and leave the victim held until released; it now flies (`ponytail:` 92's first three displays don't push, body kind 2 at PC 1097, where 136's do). Response 49, from his chuck, names 'GIANT!! MUSIC ATTACK' (133). It loops: Ryuko's each pass clears variables and rolls rand % 31 > 1 to jump past sound 16 (PCs 1798–1807), and Giant's own loops a 4-tick display that spawns love heart (440) and deletes objects 6, 8 and 9 (PCs 1944–1947). The match runs each pass's opening records itself, rolls included (`Match.musicAttack`), draws the displays without their events and releases the victim as it does a shocked one. Response 81, from counter hit, names 'Chuck 消える' (143), which deletes objects 7–9, clears its body boxes and shows nothing for 100 ticks (PCs 1957–1964), then enters rambu AIR (122, PC 1965). Op `0x07` now replaces a held player's display whatever its action, as in the source, so Counter hit's hold shows the vanished victim (PC 4334). **Moves into his own reactions:** Chuck hit (220) grabs with response 30, holds, applies 49 (PC 3268) and jumps to SINGING START (222), whose four verses (600 ticks) jump back (PC 3347). It then spawns call music explor (228), a stage-wide 50-damage box with response 43, applies 59 (stun, PC 3290) and jumps into his own FLY NO HIT (101, PC 3291). K3 hit (197) ends in his own HISA (84, PC 2843) and Drop P lend (340) in TWIS HIT + 12 (90, PC 5137), past its stops and power. `flatten` now ends a move's timeline at a jump into one of the player's reactions, as it did past action 250; `export_fighter` checks that any offset stays before the reaction's first display; and the match enters any player's own reaction by number as the port's action, as Ryuko's Musashi finish enters FLY NO HIT (97) (`ponytail:` as there, the reaction's op `0x15` power and op `0x1a` stops don't run). His moves run from their timelines at their levels: P1, K1, the crouching moves, air P, Hit up and the chuck 21, air K 22, specials 31 (TO EX, BASE BALL, Hammere p start, drop punch start, Rock p start, counter start), supers 41 (Air Fire, Fire Start, hariken start) and en300 start 42; Dash F 30, Dash B 0, the air dashes 254 and the double jumps 255, as Ryuko's. En300's handen (381) calls 382 ten times and 383 nineteen times, then a last box: 39 hits of 5 and one of 15, 210 in all. His EX mode is variable 0 at 100: --TYPE-- (entry 9: ↓, neutral, ↓, Hit Up) enters TO EX (231), which spends 50 power (PCs 3397–3404) and sets it (PC 3408), or enters TO NORMAL (232, PC 3405), which clears it (PC 3421). In EX mode op `0x1f` checks of variable 0 swap P3 for NEW P3 (191, PC 2604), K3 for New K3 (201, PC 2771), Hit up for newhIT up (216, PC 3137), BASE BALL for NEW HARIKEN start (252, PC 3434), the hammer for EX Crush (258, PC 3894), Rock p for B FIRE (274, PC 4061), Air Fire for SUPER DROP PUNCH (336, PC 4639), Fire Start for SC MAHA P (328, PC 4667) and hariken for SBB start (350, PC 5200); his HUD face (74) cycles as Ryuko's (PCs 690–707). SBB start calls SBB random and SBB RANDOM02 nine times (op `0x0b`, PCs 5333–5341), each rolling rand % 5 > 1 for which of two balls to spawn (PCs 5358, 5392). `flatten` used to keep such a roll as a branch, which lost the caller after one pitch. HID START enters FIRE BALL START (214) at 153 life or more (op `0x11`, PC 3749). chuck jump in (263) leaps at raw 750/−1400 (PC 3586) with a grab box of kind `0x10` near the apex (PC 3594, response 30), which skips a grounded victim, so it catches only one in the air: chuck sp hit (264) holds and hides it (response 81), and chuck hit lend (273) strikes it for 60 (response 63). d sp start (245) leaps with a 5-damage box (response 33, the shock); its hit branch lands and spawns dragon up handen (247, PC 3391), which rises with a 690-pixel-wide box that does no damage and has no op `0x17`. The engine skips the reaction for response 0 (`0x40f527`), but a box without damage still meets the victim: it turns the victim (`0x40f73c`) and queues an object's hit handler (`0x40f885`; the guard handler for a guard, `0x40f6e6`). So 247 enters call bird st (248) at once, which calls nine birds (249, PCs 3411–3427) down on the victim, 115 damage in all; meeting no one, 247 runs out into 248 after 60 ticks (PC 3406). en3 start's shot (288) bursts into SHOOT OBJ EXPLO (290), which clears its handlers with action 0 (PCs 3940–3942; `0x40f6e6` tests the slot) before striking for 60, twenty 6s and 20: 201 with the shot. An object's handler of action 0 now clears its slot, as a fighter's did; Amiro's 351–369 and Chung's 322 also clear theirs before striking. An object that runs out after a display now runs its records past it (`0x4125ea`), as stage objects did. Results: 勝ち (60) cues sound 10 (PC 628) and calls 'win RP' (61) ten times (PC 629) before its displays, so its clip is its flattened timeline: DEMO STAND (29, PC 627), the ten RPs, then image 409 held (PC 638). The draw calls 29 once (PC 719). Intro: 開始時 (31) jumps to 開始時 BR (33) for global 3 = 0 (PC 329), past its display for global 14 = 0 (PC 365). At age 2 he rises unseen at raw −4000 for two ticks (PC 372), stops (PC 374), and at age 104 leaps at raw 400/−1000 with gravity 60 (PC 377). 62 ticks later he is past the floor, and the landing (`0x41187f`) enters OP lend (36, slot 1 at PC 383) at age 166, cutting the display at PC 385 a tick short: 2.16 seconds in all, starting 372 arena pixels back so that he lands where Ryuko's walk ends. 開始時 BR also spawns OP super Charge (42, PC 369), which takes 300 power (op `0x15`, PC 480; `0x40e6f0` borrows stocks, down to none) and calls OP super ChargeRP (43) a hundred times for 2 each (PC 481), so he starts every Vs. CPU round with two stocks. HUD face 73 shows 2001 (PC 764); *name (69) shows 217 (PC 727); darkening image 1009 (46). His CPU writes every command into his history; CPU DASH and CPU DJ (entries 19–20) enter 352 and 353. His hit effects test his variable 2 (PCs 4112, 4124, 4619), which P3 and K3 OBJ set to 10 (PCs 2389, 2593) and the chuck hit lends to 20 (PCs 3704, 3719): see ALEX's REFLACT for why an object reads its player's variables. A second chuck super within the 200 ticks of 技名3 (277, PC 3701) sets variable 7 to 10 (265, PC 3625) for chuck hit lend 2 (274: 50 damage, response 17, PC 3700); the export now evaluates variable 7's tests (added for DIDO), so that path follows the source, though the opponent is still down when LIZARD recovers, so no test reaches it. `ponytail:` chuck hit lend sets global 0 to 10 for its strike's tick (PCs 3703, 3712); in the source, a KO by it with LIZARD as player 2, who steps after the victim, enters 'make hikari' (PC 590), a vanishing fall the port doesn't play, so the victim takes the normal KO. Authored keys: supers HIDDEN, chuck sup, dragon sp and en300 on 1–4; Cross, FIRE and jump att on U, I and F.

- **CPU:** routine `0x411270` (control type 1, case `0x4112f9`) plays the CPU fighter's own table: 100 slots of 111 bytes after the held-pose table, 43 used for Ryuko, 44 for Zoma, 43 for Kiki, 40 for Chung, 37 for Amiro, 45 for ANMC P, 43 for Giant, 41 for G Suneo, 41 for Yo and 42 for SIN. Each entry holds flags (bit 0 self airborne, bit 1 opponent airborne), a probability, a distance window in source pixels, and up to ten steps of direction, command and duration. While no entry plays, a timer counts down. At zero it resets to `rand % (101 − L) − L + 50`, every slot draws `rand % 100`, and the last entry that passes its probability, distance and airborne checks plays. Each step lasts its duration plus `rand % (101 − L)` ticks, plus one. The routine runs at the start of its fighter's step (`0x411867`, in `0x411810` before the landing check and the command scan), which a step entering a queued action, counting down a stop or held by op `0x07` (`+0x40` at −1, set at `0x41303a` until op `0x14` clears it) skips (`0x412327`, `0x412390`, `0x412397`): its timers then hold and draw nothing, and its input word that tick is player 2's keys as `0x4146d0` recorded them, none. It clears its input history each time (`0x403300`) and writes the step's direction, plus, for a command step, the command's whole input with that direction added (`0x411644`), so a command repeats through its step and entries such as down + punch attack crouching. When a hit reaches the CPU free (`+0x15e & 0xc` is 0: standing, walking, crouching, turning, or in a jump from takeoff), `0x40f3a2` guards on `rand % 100 < L`, ignoring its input (`0x40f3c2`); in a guard reaction (12) it keeps guarding, whichever way it faces (`0x40f449`). Ryuko's and Zoma's `+0x7cb6` (2) has bit 2, which lets that guard happen in the air (`0x40f475`). A CPU attacking, dashing, double jumping, falling after an air attack or landing (4), or reeling (8) takes the hit without a roll. Command 22 enters CPU IORI: action 410 (damage 10) jumps to 411 (damage 15). Op `0x20` at PC 5887 then recovers one time in six (`rand % 6 > 4`: 411 + 31, three 5-tick poses) and otherwise jumps to IORI 3 (248). Its op `0x1e` records have mode 0, which the cancel check at `0x41193a` ignores, so it cannot be cancelled. Zoma's CPU writes each command itself, as the engine does, every tick of its step, with the step's direction in every word (`Input.command`, injected by `Match.record`), since his CPU-only entries have no keys: 22 CPU DASHF (341: a dash at raw 680, then 200), 23 CPU DASHB (342), whose only CPU entry needs him airborne where it has no air action, so it never plays, and 24 cpu dash jump (343), the dash's start jumping into HIGH JUMP (6). Their steps end in button `0x40`, which no player can press. `rand` is MSVC's (`0x417a22`). Authored: level 50 (the story's value is not recovered), a separate seed per match, Ryuko's commands as the port's single buttons pressed on alternate ticks, and the neutral states (those where the player's own guard would start). Zoma's intro follows Ryuko's path: action 28 jumps to 30 + 3 for global 3 = 0 (PCs 265, 301, past its 50-tick display), which jumps to Start N (31) + 2 (PC 306), as Ryuko's 32 + 3 jumps to 33 + 4. Start N shows 11 displays over 200 ticks with voices 19 and 20 (PCs 312, 320), but no op `0x01`, so he starts 180 pixels from the stage centre, where Ryuko's walk ends, and no op `0x16`, so holding down starts no EX mode.
- **Scope:** ten fighters, 18 stages, local single-player. No full gameplay VM, original-game parity, gamepad, multiplayer, or public deployment.

## Recovered contact timing

A strike's active/inactive cycle uses **action ticks**. Successful contact adds **hit-stop**: the fighter's action age, airborne age, position, velocity, and acceleration stop advancing for the specified ticks. This is separate from the victim's subsequent hit/block recovery. One tick is 10 ms in this port. Whiffs add no pause. Effects, the round clock and the input history continue, so chain and cancel presses made during the pause count once it ends, while still in their windows. After a KO or time-out the round phase stays 1 (only a debug key sets 2, `0x406231` and `0x408f38`): moves, objects, commands and the CPU run on, and each fighter takes its result's action once back in neutral (`0x410ff1`); only the timer stops (`0x408e50`).

| Attack                                                     | Successful-hit pause per strike              | Block pause          | Source evidence                                                                          |
| ---------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- | -------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Punch 1 / Kick 1                                           | 10 ticks                                     | 10                   | Responses 1–2 → actions 73–74                                                            |
| Punch 2                                                    | 10, 10, 15                                   | 10                   | Action 180, PCs 2400/2413; responses 8–11                                                |
| Punch 3 / Kick 2 / crouching punch / aerial punch and kick | 15                                           | 10                   | Actions 181/188/185/182/191; strong/launch responses                                     |
| Hit Up                                                     | Ground: attacker 33, victim 10; air: 13 each | 10                   | Action 200; response 19 → 81, airborne response 17 → 80                                  |
| IORI                                                       | 15, 15, then ground 13 / air 15              | 10                   | Actions 246–248; responses 3/4, 71, 18/77 → 75/76, 121, 80/123                           |
| Crouching kick                                             | 13                                           | 10                   | Response 7 → action 79 (sweep)                                                           |
| Ground Drill Punch                                         | 15 for strikes 1–5; 13 for strike 6          | 10                   | Action 207, PCs 2920/2941; responses 10–11 → 75–76, 18 → 80                              |
| Kick 3                                                     | 0                                            | 0                    | Action 190, PC 2631; responses 20–21 → 83–84, 34–36 → 163/165/167                        |
| Aerial drill                                               | Attacker 8, victim 9                         | Attacker 8, victim 3 | Actions 209–211 contact branches, PCs 2989/3000, 3019–3020, 3040–3041, 3061–3062         |
| Axe object                                                 | 15 (FB → 75), airborne 15; Ryuko too         | 10                   | Action 227, PC 3288; responses 50 → 110 → 75, 4 → 76, 25–27                              |
| EX Fire shots                                              | 15 (FB → 75), full charge 13; Ryuko too      | 10                   | Objects 233/240; responses 50 → 110 → 75, 4 → 76, 58 → 112 → 80, 25–27                   |
| Getter Beam and super beam objects                         | Victim 0, finish 13; Ryuko and her beams too | 0                    | Objects 269/282/303/324 and EFS's 336/337; responses 51 → 111 → 83, 58 → 112 → 80, 34–36 |
| Super grab finish and hidden-super strikes                 | 13 each, or 0 for rapid strikes              | 10                   | Response 43 → 97, 17/18 → 80, 20 → 83; guards 78–80 → 168–172                            |

Each pause comes from the selected reaction's own `0x1a`, so stance-dependent responses (overheads, air guards) change the pause with the reaction. Aerial drill's hit and guard handlers enter the tick after contact and stop it eight ticks, the last of which moves, then two more ticks reach the next stage's box: 11 ticks per stage. Its victim resumes at different times on hit versus block, so subsequent contacts depend on their relative positions, not a fixed hit count.

Ground drill still strikes at action ages 23, 29, 35, 41, 47, 53. With every strike connecting immediately, the actual contact gaps are now **210 ms on hit** or **160 ms on block**, versus **60 ms for Kick 3**. Punch 2's first two gaps are 160 ms, and aerial kick's are 230 ms. The final strike's pause delays recovery rather than adding another strike.

Evidence: the character container tail contains 23 command entries of 82 bytes, followed by 83 four-byte response mappings at file offset `0x1576d9d`. Opcode `0x17` selects a mapping, and reaction opcode `0x1a` adds its first two bytes to the self/other fighter stop counters. The original engine handles this at `0x413637`/`0x4136ae`; `0x412390` skips animation advancement and `0x40f963` skips motion while stopped. The KGT base hit/guard stop bytes loaded at `0x43a299`/`0x43a29a` are both zero. This is static recovery from the supplied files, not original Windows playback verification. The other stop reaches every other player, so an object's hit stops its owner as a body hit does. Both stops also reach each stopped player's objects that follow it (op `0x04` flag `0x20`, `+0x28` bit `0x20000000`: Ryuko's beams 269, 282 and 324 and their glows, Zoma's 211, 242 and 245), which skip their script step and motion but keep to their parent (`0x40f992`); other objects, like the axe, run on.
